## FMOD sound banks by direct port/reverse engineering (2026-11-17)
FMOD ships as a closed binary (`libfmodstudio` 2.03.09, reached through `fmod.javafmodJNI`) and the repository holds no source or format documentation, so there is nothing to port line by line: the bank format in `crates/zomboid-engine/src/native/fmod/` (`riff.rs`, `bank.rs`, `strings.rs`, `events.rs`, `fsb.rs`, `studio.rs`) and the `FMODManager.init` tables in `src/fmod/fmod/fmod_manager.rs` are reverse engineered from `media/sound/banks/Desktop/{ZomboidSound.strings,ZomboidMusic,ZomboidSound,ZomboidSoundMP}.bank` (and `radio.bank`, which `init()` does not load) and every claim is an oracle diff against the game's OWN `FMODManager.init()` running headless with the game's natives (`PZ_NATIVES`, the Steam macOS `Contents/Java`), `FmodBanksProbe` (`tests/original_probe`) against `pz-dump fmod*`. The banks (1.3 GB) are read with ranged reads; the sample data is never loaded.
`sh tools/oracle.sh fmodbanks` -> `OK 209 lines identical`; `fmodstrings` -> `OK 11768`; `fmodevents` -> `OK 18542`; `fmodsamples` -> `OK 37366` (goldens `tests/golden/engine/fmod*.sha256`, tests `oracle_hashes.rs::fmod{banks,strings,events,samples}_matches_original`).

| Chunk or list | What it holds | Compared or UNKNOWN |
|---|---|---|
| RIFF `FEV ` container, `FMT `, `BNKI`, the lists of `LIST PROJ`, its tail chunks (`SNDH`, `STDT`, `STBL`, `HASH`, `DEL `, `MUTE`, `REFI`, `PLAT`), `SND ` | the file framing; `BNKI` is the bank GUID, 8 zero bytes and the event count the API reports | compared (`fmodbanks`): sizes, child counts and `LCNT` of every list in all five files, the GUIDs, the `SND ` offset and size, and the Studio bank list with `FMOD_Studio_Bank_GetEventCount`/`GetVCACount` (3215, 272, 7, 0 events; `BNKI`'s event count equals the API's). The second `BNKI` u32 (7 in every bank) and the two `FMT ` words are UNKNOWN |
| `STDT` of `ZomboidSound.strings.bank` | the string table: a trie (24-bit node links, case-preserving labels, case-insensitive lookup) from every path to its GUID and back, 3 tables for the lookups | compared (`fmodstrings`): the 3494 event paths and GUIDs (3490 distinct paths) per bank in API order, the 17 VCA paths, `FMODManager.getEventPathList` (sorted with `compareToIgnoreCase`), the 117 parameter names, and `FMOD_Studio_System_GetBus`/`GetVCA` over 4644 candidate paths (every string, its lower and upper case, one character more and less, paths that name nothing) |
| `EVTS` (`EVTB`) | per event: GUID, timeline GUID, bus GUIDs, parameter link GUIDs and parameter ids | the event list is the `EVNT` entries that have a path in the string table (the other 841, 107 and 71 entries are event instruments that no API call lists); the flag in `EVTB` that tells the two apart is not decoded: UNKNOWN, the rule "has a path" is what is compared (`BNKI` agrees in all banks). The numeric head fields (13 bytes at 80) and the 20 bytes after the link vector are UNKNOWN |
| `TLNS` (`TLNB`, `LIST TRNS`/`TRAN`: `TRNB`, `TRTL`) | per event the timeline: five arrays (module instances, a second module array, sustain points, markers and regions, tempo markers) and the transitions | compared (`fmodevents`): `HasSustainPoint` (sustain array not empty) and `GetLength` of all 3494 events: the longest end of module instances (start+length), sustain positions, markers (position plus the length of an unnamed region) and, for a marker that is the destination of a transition with a transition timeline, position plus that timeline's length in samples; samples are 48 kHz, milliseconds rounded down. Found by loading patched copies of `ZomboidMusic.bank` in the game's natives (the length followed a marker's position, +1000 ms for exactly the transition destination; tempo markers and transition fields changed nothing). The tempo array and the other fields are UNKNOWN |
| `PMLS` (`PMLB`), `PRMS` (`PRMB`) | the link from an event to a parameter (its GUID) and the parameter record (GUID, two kind words, name, range) | compared (`fmodevents`): the parameter list of every event in API order with name and flags, the 43 global parameters of `GetParameterDescriptionList` (all banks, once by GUID, in GUID order), `FMODManager.parameterDescriptionMap` (117 entries, `globalIndex` = insertion order: globals first, then in the order of the events of the banks). Flags: bit 0 of the first kind word is global (4), bit 3 discrete (8), bit 5 labeled (16), bit 4 built-in (readonly+automatic, 3); a global parameter inside an event reads as readonly too (4 -> 5). Measured on the 10 kind pairs the banks hold |
| parameter id (`FMOD_STUDIO_PARAMETER_ID`, 8 bytes) | stored per event in `EVTB` | compared for the 113 parameters that an event uses (read from the record). The 4 global parameters no event uses (`MusicToggleMute`, `WorldEvents`, `SnowIntensity`, `_RainSurfaceHardness`) have no id in any bank (0 occurrences of the 8 bytes in the three files): it is a hash of something; CRC-32 (zlib, C, JAM, bzip2, MPEG-2), Murmur2/3 (32-bit with seeds, 64A, x64-128), xxHash32/64, FNV-1/1a 32 and 64, djb2, sdbm, one-at-a-time, SuperFastHash, lookup3, Adler-32, MD5/SHA-1/SHA-256/SHA-512/BLAKE2 prefixes were tried over the name, lower and upper case, `parameter:/Name` and the GUID in several forms and none gives the id of `CameraZoom`. UNKNOWN; the probe and `pz-dump` print `UNKNOWN` for those 4 |
| `VCAS`, `IBSS`/`GBSS`/`MBSS`/`RBSS` buses | VCA and bus records | the VCA GUIDs and paths are compared; bus and VCA lookups are by the path prefix. The records' contents (volumes, effects, routing), `BEFX`/`PEFX`/`SEFX`/`SCFX`/`SSFX`, `CTRS`, `CRVS`, `MPGS`, `MUIS`, `SPIS`, `PRIS`, `EVIS`, `WAIS`, `EFIS`, `CMDS`, `SLNS`, `LWVS`, `WAVS`, `SNAS`, `MODS`, `SNDH`, `STBL`, `HASH`: counted and sized by `fmodbanks`, content not decoded (UNKNOWN meaning) |
| `SND ` = FSB5 | zero padding, then an FSB5 file: 60-byte header (version 1, sample count, sample header size, name table size, data size, codec 15 = Vorbis), one 8-byte header per stream with chunks, the name table, the sample data | compared (`fmodsamples`): header numbers and names are the probe's own read (FMOD core has no call that returns names), and per stream (318 + 15102 + 3107 + 152) FMOD core opening the same head reports the stream count, frequency, channels (2 bits: 1, 2, 6; chunk 14 gives 4), PCM length, ms length (rounded down), and format (FSB type 20, decoded PCM float, 32 bits). Vorbis decoding is NOT ported: FMOD looks the setup header up by the CRC-32 in each stream's chunk 11 (3 distinct values in all banks) inside its library; chunk 13 (a float) and the loop chunk are UNKNOWN. Codecs other than 15, frequency chunks and channel code 3 are not in the banks: the port returns `Err` for them |

Measured, and different from what a first reading of the files gives:
- The Studio API counts only the `EVNT` entries with a path (see the table); `FMOD_Studio_Bank_GetEventList` order is the GUID-sorted order of the bank, and a later bank's event with the same lower-cased path replaces an earlier one in `eventDescriptionMap` while `eventPathList` keeps both (4 paths occur in two banks).
- `FMODManager.initGlobalParameters` runs before the events: `globalIndex` 0-42 are the globals in GUID order, the event-local parameters follow in the order the first event of the first bank mentions them.
- `FMOD_System_CreateSound` of the native shim prints the new sound's address with C `printf` on stdout; the probe points descriptor 1 at `/dev/null` around that call.

NOT ported / UNKNOWN: audio playback itself (`FMOD_System_Init` output, event instances, DSP graph, the 3D engine), Vorbis decoding (setup headers inside the library), the bank GUID hashes (`HASH`), the events' numeric head fields, the buses' and effects' contents, the parameter id hash, `radio.bank` as a loaded bank (only its FSB5 and container are compared).

## Player body and needs by direct port (2026-11-17)
`sh tools/oracle.sh body` -> `OK   body: 738 lines identical` (golden hash `tests/golden/engine/body.sha256`, test `oracle_hashes.rs::body_matches_original`; `BodyProbe` in the booted dedicated server against `pz-dump body`). The ORIGINAL runs on a headless `IsoPlayer` (no client, no animation, no inventory contents): `Nutrition.update`, `IsoGameCharacter.calculateStats`, `Moodles.Update` and `BodyDamage.Update` are ticked N times per scenario (`tests/body_cases.txt`, spec `seed:ticks:run:every:show[:options]`), and the probe prints every stat, the nutrition state, the moodle levels (with the thermoregulation chevrons), the trait list, the draw count of `RandStandard`, the thermoregulator and its 17 nodes, every body part (pain and 40 fields) and the body state, floats as the hex of their raw bits. Scenario options set the world (`mpd`, `mult`, `tod`, `nights`, `sb.<SandboxOption>`, the climate floats `temp wind daylight night rain humidity`), the player (`stat.<Id>`, nutrition, traits, perks, `moving running sprint sneak asleep sit bed delay sleep`, `zombies`, the square: `room`, `sqfield`) and inject any Java field of a part, the body, the character, the thermoregulator or a node by name (`bp.<i>.<field>`, `bd.`, `ch.`, `th.`, `node.<i>.`; no setter runs, so no random draw is spent).

| Part of the original | Ported in | Compared or blocker |
|---|---|---|
| `Nutrition.update` (calories, macros, weight, weight traits), `Metabolics`, `Stats`/`CharacterStat` operations | `characters/body_damage/nutrition.rs`, `characters/stats.rs` | compared: resting, walking, running, sprinting, sneaking, asleep, with weight and macro starts; the stages 1-3 scenarios (350 lines before the body update) |
| `Moodles`, `Moodle`, `MoodleStat`, `MoodleType` (levels, chevrons, the hypo/hyperthermia colour) | `characters/moodles/` | compared on every tick of the scenarios (the level of every moodle type) |
| `IsoGameCharacter.calculateStats` chain: endurance, thirst, stress, awake and sleeping stats, morale, fitness, `Temperature`/air temperature for a square (`ClimateManager.getAirTemperatureForCharacter`, wind chill), `ZomboidGlobals` from `defines.lua` | `body_damage/body_sim.rs`, `iso/weather/`, `zomboid_globals.rs` | compared |
| `BodyDamage.Update` (health, pain, infection, bleeding, moodle damage, boredom, discomfort, strength, illness, panic, temperature state, `UpdateWetness`, `UpdateCold`), `BodyPart.DamageUpdate`/`getPain`/`getAdditionalPain`, `calculateOverallBodyHealth`, mortality draws of the infection | `body_damage/body_update.rs`, `body_part.rs` | compared: scratch, bite (modes 1, 3, 4, 7 of the mortality sandbox), laceration, deep wound unbandaged / bandaged / stitched, alcohol, garlic, plantain, fracture with a splint, burn, infected wounds, fake infection, a cold, hunger, thirst, poison, food sickness, intoxication, panic with zombies in view, smoker, sleep on the floor |
| `Thermoregulator` (`update`, set point, core rate of change, metabolic rate, nodes heat deltas, insulation of a node, body multipliers, thermal damage up to death by cold) | `body_damage/thermoregulator.rs` | compared at 17 nodes: rest, cold, very cold, hypothermia and cold death, hot, hyperthermia, sleep, walking, running, sprinting, sneaking, the strength perk, rain outdoors and indoors, wet cold |
| `ClothingWetness.updateWetness` of a character wearing nothing | `body_update.rs` (`clothing_wetness_update`) | compared (perspiration and outer wetness of the body parts) |
| `BodyPart` storage, `BodyPartType`, `Fitness`, `Metabolics` | `body_part.rs`, `body_part_type.rs`, `fitness.rs`, `metabolics.rs` | the part fields and the metabolic targets are compared through the scenarios; `BodyPartContacts`/`BodyPartLast` are not ported (clothing and combat only), the fitness exercise actions are not exercised |
| `BodyDamage.splatBloodFloor` | `body_update.rs` | the random draws only (`isDead && Next(10)`, `Next(14)`, `Next(50)`); the chunk blood-splat list is not modelled, the body does not read it |

Measured, and different from what a reading of the decompiled text gives:
- `decompiled/` `Thermoregulator.getSimulationMultiplier` drops the `GameTime.getMultiplier()` factor. The bytecode (`javap -p -c`) multiplies `multiplier * constant * simulationMultiplier`; the port follows the bytecode (the first healthy-player scenario diverged at tick 1 until then).
- The booted dedicated server has the sandbox `Nutrition` true, `MuscleStrainFactor` 0.7 and `WoundInfectionFactor` 1.0, not the table defaults; `pz-dump body` applies them before the scenario's own `sb.*` and prints a `SANDBOX` line from both sides so such a difference is visible. `GameServer.server` is true, so `AdjustForFramerate(chance)` is `(int)(chance * 0.33333334F)`; `updateBandages` does nothing on the server; no Lua handler of `OnPlayerGetDamage` exists in the shared or server Lua.
- `IsoPlayer.getHoursSurvived()` is the player's own `hoursSurvived` field (0 headless), not the world age. `IsoGameCharacter.isDead()` reads `health <= 0 || bodyDamage.getHealth() <= 0` live, so a dead player's moodles reset to level 0 (`sync_dead`). `getWeightMod` of perk level 0 is 0.8, so the headless `maxWeight` is `(int)(8 * 0.8) = 6` minus reducers, times `maxWeightDelta`. The `GameTime` multiplier of `mult=1` is 0.8; the probe clock starts at 9.0, night 0.

NOT ported / UNKNOWN (each returns `Err(not ported ...)` in the port when a scenario would reach it, never a guessed value):
- Worn clothing: `Thermoregulator.updateClothing` and `ThermalNode.calculateInsulation` read `ItemVisuals`, `Clothing.getInsulation/getWindresistance/getWaterResistance/getCurrentCondition/getWetness`, `BloodClothingType.getCoveredParts` and `ItemVisual.getHole`; `ClothingWetness` layers the worn items per body part and calls `Clothing.updateWetness` (world age, `getWetDryState`); `updateDiscomfortModifiers`, `getCorpseSicknessDefense`, `hasDirtyClothing`/`hasBloodyClothing` of `BodyPart.DamageUpdate` read the worn list. The port has no worn-items container, no `ItemVisual`/`BloodClothingType` model and `inventory/types/clothing.rs` is a save struct; the node's `ClothingLayer` list is only a place for them. The scenarios of "cold/hot weather with different clothing" therefore run naked (the `ch.has_worn_clothing` flag returns `not ported`). The decompiled sources and the script values (for example `Jacket_Padded` insulation 0.95, wind resistance 0.95, water resistance 0.6, blood location `Jumper;FullHelmet;Neck`) are available, so this is the next step, not a native blocker.
- `TriggerSneezeCough` (a world sound with an `IsoGameCharacter` emitter), god mode, burning to death, vehicles (`UpdateWetness` in a car), tents and beds as objects (the bed type is a probe flag), the weight-carried damage of `BodyDamage.Update` (inventory weight; `capw` is read from the headless inventory), corpse sickness (`CorpseCount` chunk data; the probe reports 0), `Hemophobic` (`getTotalBlood`), the Lua events of the body, blood on clothing and floors (`addBlood`, visuals), eating (`JustAteFood` reads `Food` items; only the `Nutrition` side is compared), the attacking metabolic target (`WeaponType`), `Thermoregulator_tryouts.java` (not ported by decision).

## Inventory items and containers by direct port (2026-11-18)
`sh tools/oracle.sh inventory` prints `OK` (golden hash `tests/golden/engine/inventory.sha256`, test `inventory_matches_original` in `crates/zomboid-engine/tests/oracle_hashes.rs`). `tests/original_probe/InventoryProbe.java` runs the ORIGINAL `InventoryItemFactory`, `InventoryItem`, `ItemContainer`, `Food` and `DrainableComboItem` inside the booted server (`ServerHarness`), with `GameServer.server` cleared while it runs: the items' `update()` reads the game clock in single-player (`calculateTimeMultiplier`), a server reads `System.currentTimeMillis()`, which no port can reproduce. `pz-dump inventory` prints the same lines from `crates/zomboid-engine/src/zombie/inventory/runtime/` (`item.rs` the items, `world.rs` the arenas and what an operation reads from the rest of the game, `container.rs` `ItemContainer`, `food.rs` `Food`), an arena `World` of items and containers addressed by index. Floats print as raw bits and `Float.toString`; the generator is `java.util.Random(seed)` with `RandAbstract.id` counted, so every random draw of an operation is compared too.

| What | Class and method | Result |
|---|---|---|
| every item script made as an object (`create`, stage 1) | `InventoryItemFactory.CreateItem`, `Item.InstanceItem` (class constructor, class branch setters, common setters, `synchWithVisual` with its draw of the model index, the factory's `id = Rand.Next(2146250223) + 1233423`, a drainable's full `setCurrentUses`) | 2031 of 5092 scripts (921 `ComboItem`, 146 `DrainableComboItem`, 701 `Food`, 263 `Literature`): class, category, `getActualWeight`/`getWeight`/`getUnequippedWeight`/`getEquippedWeight` (the equipped multiplier is the real `defines.lua` value), condition, count and tags identical |
| container operations (stage 2) | `ItemContainer`: `AddItem(String)` (obsolete and unknown scripts give null, a food takes the container temperature), `AddItem(InventoryItem)` (an id already held gives the held item; the old container's `Remove`; a food's `OnAddedToContainer` ages it), `Remove(item)`, `Remove(String)`, `RemoveAll`, `RemoveOneOf`, `contains`/`containsTypeRecurse`/`findItem` (`ignoreBroken`, `/` alternatives, `Type:Food`), `getCountType`, `getAllType`, `getAllTag`, `getAllCategory`, `getCountTag`, `getFirstType/Category/Tag`, `getSomeType`, `getBestCondition` (the first of equals, null at condition 0), `getNumberOfItem`, `containsWithModule`, `FindAndReturnStack`, `hasRoomFor`, `isItemAllowed` (`OnlyAcceptCategory`), `getCapacityWeight`/`getContentsWeight`/`getMaxWeight`/`getCapacity` (capped at 100)/`getFreeCapacity`/`isFull`/`getAvailableWeightCapacity`, `getTemperature` (custom, the fridge shut-off warming) | identical in 7 scenarios (items of many scripts, capacity, `OnlyAcceptCategory`, removal, best condition, drainable weights) |
| `Food` state as it reports it | `getHungerChange`, `getThirstChange`, `getUnhappyChange`, `getBoredomChange`, `getStressChange`, `getEnduranceChange`, `getFoodSicknessChange`, `isRotten`, `isFresh` by age, cooked, burnt, frozen; `getCurrentUses`/`getMaxUses`/`setCurrentUses`/`consumeHunger`/`multiplyFoodValues`; `setCooked`, `setBurnt`, `setFreezingTime`; `DrainableComboItem.setCurrentUsesFloat`/`updateWeight` | identical |
| `Food.updateAge`, `updateFreezing` | the clock, `getFoodRotSpeed`, `getFridgeFactor`, `ElecShutModifier`, the heat's lerp to the container temperature, thawing in 1.5 h (/6 above 1.0), frozen items do not age | identical over 6 scenarios (default clock, fridge, freezer, hot container, fridge with the power shut off, rot speed 1, fridge factor 5, thawing) |
| time and use (stage 3) | `Food.update` (`calculateTimeMultiplier`, `ALREADY_COOKED`, `updateTemperature`, cooking by the minute with the container's temperature, tainted clean-up, `replaceOnCooked`, `setCooked`, `removeUnhappinessWhenCooked`, the rice/pasta restart, `removeNegativeEffectOnCooked`, `BadInMicrowave`, `burnt`, the tainted branch), `Food.updateRotting` (`replaceOnRotten` through `CreateItem(type, food)`, `DaysForRottenFoodRemoval`, `destroyThisItem`), `DrainableComboItem.update` (heat, cooking, `ticksPerEquipUse` uses, removal and `replaceOnDeplete`, `updateTowelWetness`) and `Use`, `InventoryItem.update` (wet cooldown -> `itemWhenDry`, `itemHeat`), `InventoryItem.Use`, `ItemUser.UseItem`, `copyConditionStatesFrom` | identical over 9 more scenarios (`tick <item> <n> <dtod>` moves the clock and calls `update()` n times): wet towels and cloths drying (two multipliers), `Icecream` rotting into `IcecreamMelted`, `DaysForRottenFoodRemoval` removing a rotten apple, a steak cooked then burnt in an oven, `BreadSlices` -> `Toast`, `BaguetteDough` -> `Baguette`, a pot in a microwave, tainted food cleaned by heat, batteries/towels/candles used up (`replaceOnDeplete`, `useWhileUnequipped`), `Use` and `UseItem` on food, bandages (`replaceOnUse`) and bullets, items outside any container cooling to 1.0 |
| save and load (stage 4) | `InventoryItem.save`/`load`/`loadItem` (the registry id, the `Byte` and `Integer` `BitHeader`s with every flag: uses, condition, custom colour, `itemCapacity`, modData, activated, repairs, name, `byteData`, extra items, custom name and weight, key id, remote id and range, colour, worker, wet cooldown, favourite, stash map, infection, ammo, attached slot and model, max capacity, recorded media, world scale and rotation, model index, `isInitialised`; the placeholder of an empty second header is dropped), `Food.save`/`load` (nutrition, hunger, heat, cooking, spices, chef, off ages, reductions, freezing, rotting, milk, stress), `Literature.save`/`load` (pages, custom pages, `lockedBy`, learned recipes), `DrainableComboItem` (the uses as saved by `InventoryItem`), `KahluaTableImpl.save`, `ItemContainer.save`/`load` and `CompressIdenticalItems.save`/`load` (types in first-seen order, identical consecutive items compared by their bytes saved with id 0 and by `byteData`, the explored and looted flags and the capacity), `GameWindow.WriteString`, `WorldDictionary.getItemRegistryID`/`getItemTypeFromID`; the Rust write buffer `ByteBufferWriter` and `BitHeaderWrite` (`java_nio.rs`) | identical, case `inventory` now 12918 lines: the registry id of all 5092 scripts (`regs`); every one of the 2030 modelled scripts made, saved, loaded back and saved again (`createsave`: the bytes, the item that came back with every field `fields` reads, and the second save); each flag of the writers set in turn on a `ComboItem`, a `DrainableComboItem`, a `Food` and a `Literature` and round-tripped (condition, colours, modData with nested and number keys, and so on); containers with identical, differing and `byteData`-carrying items (`csave`/`cload`: the bytes, the loaded items, the bytes again, the bytes the loader consumed, the generator draws). The two directions are one check: the bytes the port writes are the bytes the original writes at every step, so the loader of the original and the loader of the port read the same input and the state they end in (and what they write again) is compared line by line |
| the same code on the bytes the game wrote (stage 5, case `invbytes`) | `ItemContainer.load`/`save`, `InventoryItem.loadItem`/`saveWithSize` over every container and item block the port's chunk, vehicle and player loaders find in `profile/Saves/Sandbox/2026-11-07_22-34-13` (`pz-dump invcorpus` keeps the bytes each loader read in `raw` of the old `ItemContainer`/`InventoryItem` and writes them as `rawcont`/`rawitem` cases into `target/oracle/invbytes.cases`; nothing of the save is committed) | identical, case `invbytes` 3230 lines: 610 container ranges and 2321 item blocks, each loaded by the ORIGINAL class in the booted server and by the port, the loaded state printed (`show` and `fields`), and saved again. Of those, 144 containers and 1114 items are of the modelled classes: ALL of them save again byte for byte equal to the bytes the game wrote (`same=1`, 0 differences), so the writers are right on data the game itself wrote, EXCEPT the modData entry `customName`, which this check does NOT cover: the loading game rewrites it in its own language (`setCustomName`), so on BOTH sides, the original and the port, the probe restores the `customName` of the original block before saving again (see "Measured while doing it"). The rest print `SKIPPED` (1673): 488 `OnCreate` scripts (`Photo`, `Newspaper`, magazines, paperbacks, fliers, comics, fabric rolls ...), 342 scripts with components (empty cans, cups, bottles), classes `Clothing` 358, `HandWeapon` 298, `InventoryContainer` 75, `AlarmClock` 21, `Radio` 12, `Key` 6, `AlarmClockClothing` 5, `Moveable` 4, `Map` 4, `WeaponPart` 2, and 58 items that name recorded media (the save's `recorded_media.bin` indexes are loaded into the original's `RecordedMedia`; the port has no media) |

Measured while doing it:
- Registry ids (`WorldDictionary.getItemRegistryID`): the ids of a freshly booted server equal the ids in the `WorldDictionary.bin` of the player's save for all 5092 item scripts. They are the iteration order of a `HashMap<String,ItemInfo>` numbered from 0 (`java::hash_map_order`), equal buckets in the order the scripts were first met while parsing: the script files sorted with `template_` first, then by path, the definition order inside a file (kept as `ScriptManager.item_parse_order`; `manager.items` is sorted by name inside a module and does not give it).
- `KahluaTable` writes its entries in insertion order (measured with nested tables and string and number keys: `KahluaTableImpl.save` of the original and `KahluaTable::save` of the port agree), numbers as doubles.
- `InventoryItem.load` ends with `setCustomName(bits.hasFlags(64))`, which writes `customName` into modData: an item that has been loaded saves again with a modData table (`{customName=<name>}`, plus `Tooltip` when the script has one). `Item.InstanceItem` puts the script `Tooltip` and the parameters `DoParam` has no branch for (as a number when they parse as a double) into modData, `colorRed/Green/Blue` below 255 into `col`, and `isInitialised` is true on every created item.
- `InventoryItemFactory.createItemInternal` gives the poisonous berry of the world detection level 1 and power 5 and its poisonous mushroom 2 and 10; the probe fixes them (`Base.BerryGeneric2`, `Base.MushroomGeneric3`) at every `reset`.
- `Food.load` of `Base.CorpseAnimal` builds a dead animal body (`initAnimalIcons` -> `loadCorpseFromByteData`): not ported (the case prints `SKIP`); a saved recorded-media index needs the `RecordedMedia` of the world to load, only its writer is compared; the `texture` flag (268435456) is never set by the classes modelled here and the loader refuses a save that has it.
- The player's save was written by a game in Russian: `InventoryItem.load` ends with `setCustomName`, which writes the name in the language of the LOADING game into modData `customName` (`Straw` where the file holds `Соломинка`), so a loaded item saves again with the name of the loader's language and no item re-saves equal. The probe puts the name the block holds (found in its table: key type 0, `customName`, value type 0, the string) back on the item before saving, in the original and in the port alike; the check then is about every other byte, and all of them match.
- `ItemContainer.AddItem(String)` does NOT call `OnAddedToContainer`: a food added by type keeps `lastAged` and `lastFrozenUpdate` at -1 until something ages it; `AddItem(InventoryItem)` does.
- `IsoWorld.instance.currentCell` exists in the booted server: `addToProcessItems` lands, and `Food.updateAge` leaves the heat alone for an item already in the processing list (the lerp branch needs an item that is not in it).
- A container with no parent object is never powered (`isPowered` needs `parent`), so no food freezes there; a fridge or freezer holds its factor only through the `ElecShutModifier` branch (`getSourceGrid()` is null); `isFridgeOrFreezerWarming` holds on day `ElecShutModifier` before 13:00 (measured with `ElecShutModifier=0`: the fridge's temperature is 0.4666667 at 9:00, 0.6 at 10:00, 1.0 from day 1).
- `getActualWeightUnmodded` returns 0 when the display name equals the full type (the translation of an item that has none).

Blockers (the probe prints `SKIP <script> <reason>` for them and claims nothing; the count is of the 3061 skipped scripts): class `Clothing` 1395, `HandWeapon` 409, `Moveable` 383, script `OnCreate` (Lua) 333, class `InventoryContainer` (bags) 308, scripts with components (fluid containers, attributes such as `Sharpness`) 165, `Radio` 17, `AlarmClockClothing` 16, `Map` 15, `WeaponPart` 11, `Key` 6, `AlarmClock` 2, `Animal` 1. They need what the port does not model: character, visual and outfit state, the Lua functions of `OnCreate`/`OnBreak`/`OnCooked`, the component system and fluids, the devices. A Lua event (`OnContainerUpdate`) fired by `updateAge`, `destroyThisItem` and the wet item's drying has no handler in the booted server's Lua, so it is not reproduced. Time and use stop with an `UNPORTED` error (nothing is guessed) where they would reach a Lua `OnCooked`/`OnBreak` function, a player, a world item (`IsoWorldInventoryObject`), a sound, a fire or a compost; the scenarios do not reach them. The save round trip is done (stage 4, above) for the modelled classes; the blocked classes stay blocked there too (their `save` needs their own state).

## 3D models and animation clips by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh txtmodels`, `xscene`, `animscene`, `xmodelscene`, `xmodels`, `animclips`, `fbxscene`, `fbxmodelscene`, `fbxanimscene`, `fbxmodels`, `fbxanims`, `glbscene`, `glbmodelscene`, `glbanimscene`, `glbmodels` and `glbanims` print `OK` (golden hashes in `tests/golden/engine/`, tests in `crates/zomboid-engine/tests/oracle_hashes.rs`). Two kinds of code load the game's 3D data. `.txt` models and animations go through `ModelLoader.loadTxt`, pure Java, ported literally (`zombie/core/skinnedmodel/model/model_txt.rs`, `animation/*`, the LWJGL math in `org/lwjgl/util/vector/`). Everything else (`.x`, `.fbx`, `.glb`) is read by the NATIVE assimp library through `jassimp.Jassimp.importFile`; the game's `libjassimp` runs headless here (a probe imports `.x`, `.fbx` and `.glb` files without a window), so the oracle is the original library itself, never another assimp build. The reference is the x86-64 slice of `libjassimp.dylib` (assimp 5.3, libc++, zlib 1.2.13) run under Rosetta with the game's bundled x86-64 JRE (`PZ_JRE_X86`): the arm64 slice contracts multiply-add and differs in the last bit of some rotation keys. There is no assimp source in the repository, so every step was written from the algorithm and checked against the library on every game file (and its disassembly where the data could not decide: the step order, `std::sort`, the vertex hash map).

| Formats | Loader class | Pure Java or native | Compared or blocker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `media/models/*.txt` (46), `media/animsold/*.txt` (400) | `ModelLoader.loadTxt` (`ModelTxt`, `AnimationClip`, `Keyframe`, `SkinningData`, `VertexBufferObject.VertexArray`) | pure Java | compared, case `txtmodels`: 2184 loads in static/reverse/skinnedTo combinations, format, vertex floats, element indices, bones, matrices, clips, keyframes with `Float.toString`, per-bone tables, section digests |
| `media/models_X/**/*.x` (1781), `media/anims_X/**/*.x` (2209) read to an `aiScene` | `Jassimp.importFile` -> native `XFileImporter` (`XFileParser`: the text format `xof 0303txt 0032`, which every game file uses; the binary and compressed `.x` variants are not ported), `ScenePreprocessor` | native, ported literally | compared, case `xscene` (3990 files, no post-processing): meshes, positions, normals, uvs, colors, faces, bones with weights and offset matrices, node tree with transforms, animation channels with every key |
| the same files with the game's flags | `FileTask_LoadAnimation` (`MAKE_LEFT_HANDED`, `REMOVE_REDUNDANT_MATERIALS`), `FileTask_LoadMesh` (`FIND_INSTANCES`, `MAKE_LEFT_HANDED`, `LIMIT_BONE_WEIGHTS`, `TRIANGULATE`, `OPTIMIZE_MESHES`, `REMOVE_REDUNDANT_MATERIALS`, `JOIN_IDENTICAL_VERTICES`) | native, ported literally | compared, cases `animscene` (2209 files) and `xmodelscene` (1781 files) |
| `.fbx` (2032 files, 1891 binary and 141 ASCII: `WorldItems` 1609 + `Clothing` 217, `vehicles` 146, `models_X` 23, `weapons/**` 34, `IsoObject` 6, `media/textures/WorldItems` 1), animation scale 0.01 and rotate modifier | native `FBXImporter` (tokenizer, parser with inflate, document, `FBXConverter`: meshes, the `$AssimpFbx$` chain nodes, skin clusters, materials, node animations) and `TriangulateProcess` | native, ported from the library's behaviour and disassembly (`native/assimp/fbx/`, `post_process/triangulate.rs`) | compared on EVERY file, no exclusions: `fbxscene` (the importer alone: meshes, positions, normals, tangents, UV and colour sets, faces, bone weights and offsets, node tree with transforms, animation channels with every key, and with `+mat` every material property (key, semantic, index, type, bytes) - 2032 files, 21575 lines), `fbxmodelscene` (the seven mesh flags of `FileTask_LoadMesh`), `fbxanimscene` (the two animation flags), `fbxmodels` (`ProcessedAiScene.process` with the `.fbx` modifiers: `Normal`, `StaticMesh`, by name, all meshes, skinned to `Cow_Skeleton.x`) and `fbxanims` (`AnimationOnly` with the modifiers, and skinned to another skeleton). 0 files differ in any of the five cases. What is NOT ported is listed under "FBX: not ported" below |
| `.glb` (59: `IsoObject` 57, `physics` 2; 121 meshes, 53 skinned meshes, 106 animations in 53 files, one file has 20 meshes), loaded by `FileTask_LoadMesh.loadGLTF` (the seven mesh flags) and `FileTask_LoadAnimation.loadGLTF` (`MAKE_LEFT_HANDED`, `REMOVE_REDUNDANT_MATERIALS`, scale 0.01 and the rotate modifier) | native `glTF2Importer` and `glTF2::Asset` (GLB container, a JSON reader faithful to `rapidjson`'s number parsing, `LazyDict` loading order, accessors with the vertex remapping table, skins, nodes, materials, animation samplers) | native, ported from the library's behaviour and disassembly (`native/assimp/gltf2/`) | compared on EVERY file, no exclusions: `glbscene` (the importer alone, with `+mat` every material property: 59 files, 1188 lines), `glbmodelscene` (the seven mesh flags of `FileTask_LoadMesh`, 1105 lines), `glbanimscene` (the two animation flags, 1129 lines), `glbmodels` (`ProcessedAiScene.process` in five passes, 3910 lines) and `glbanims` (`AnimationOnly` with the modifiers and skinned to another skeleton, 1556 lines): byte-identical to the original library, nothing skipped. A full dump of all 59 files (23022 lines: every position, normal, UV, colour, face, bone weight, offset matrix, node transform, animation key and material property) was also compared outside the golden hashes, and six hand-made `.glb` files (scratch, not in the repository: lazy-load order, unused objects, STEP and CUBICSPLINE, TRS fallbacks, several JOINTS/WEIGHTS sets, 120 animated nodes, shuffled node trees, rapidjson number forms, interleaved strides, colour and UV sets, non-indexed and out-of-range indices, matrix nodes, a texture with and without a sampler) are identical in the same dump. What the 59 files do not reach is listed in `glTF2: not ported (E66)` below. |
| what the game does with an imported scene: `ProcessedAiScene`, `ImportedSkeleton`, `ImportedSkinnedMesh`, `ImportedStaticMesh`, `JAssImpImporter` (`FindNode`, `getMatrixFromAiMatrix`, `GetKeyFramePosition`/`Rotation`/`Scale`), `HelperFunctions.transform`, `Quaternion.mulInverse` (bone indices and hierarchy, bind/inverse bind/skin offset matrices, `AnimationClip` from node channels, vertex arrays, bounds, mesh transforms) | pure Java (`zombie/core/skinnedmodel/model/jassimp/`) | pure Java, ported literally | compared, cases `xmodels` (1781 `.x` models x 5 passes: `Normal`, `StaticMesh`, `Normal` by mesh name, `StaticMesh` with `allMeshes`, `Normal` skinned to another skeleton) and `animclips` (2209 `.x` animations x 3 passes: `AnimationOnly`, with the `.fbx` scale 0.01 and rotate modifier, skinned to another skeleton): bone indices in name order, hierarchy, the three matrix lists, every clip's keyframes and per-bone tables, vertex format and floats, element indices, bounds, mesh transforms, section digests; the original is the game's own `ProcessedAiScene.process` run by reflection |

Measured:
- Post-processing order is the registration order of `GetPostProcessingStepInstanceList` in the library (read from the disassembly): MakeLeftHanded, FlipUVs, FlipWindingOrder, RemoveVC, RemoveRedundantMaterials, EmbedTextures, FindInstances, OptimizeGraph, ..., Triangulate, ..., OptimizeMeshes, ..., JoinVertices, ..., LimitBoneWeights, ImproveCacheLocality. The order of the flags in the Java enum is not the order they run in.
- `JoinVerticesProcess` of this library is not the `SpatialSort` one: vertices are looked up in a libc++ `std::unordered_map<Vertex, int>` hashed on the position bits only (`-0.0` hashes as `0.0`, boost-style combine), compared with a squared epsilon `1e-5f * 1e-5f` over position, normal, tangent, bitangent, every texture set and color set; the map is reserved so it never rehashes, a new node goes in front of its bucket, so of two unique vertices both within the epsilon the NEWER one is found first. Vertices no face uses are dropped.
- `LimitBoneWeightsProcess` sorts a vertex's weights with libc++ `std::sort` (sort3/4/5 networks and the guarded insertion sort below 24 elements, limit read from the disassembled `__introsort`); ties keep the order that algorithm leaves. Ranges of 24 or more elements return an error in this port (no game file reaches it).
- `OptimizeMeshesProcess` joins only the meshes ONE node refers to (same vertex format, same material index, no bones, not shared by several nodes); the merge list is the later meshes first and the first mesh last, the merged name is the names joined by `.` (`Bob_GreaveSpike_L_ALT.Bob_GreaveSpike_L_ALT`); the scene's mesh array is rebuilt in node order. `FindInstances` leaves `Sandwich.X` alone: two meshes with identical geometry and different materials stay two meshes. The port checks for equal meshes with equal materials and refuses the scene (none exists in the game files).
- `MakeLeftHanded` negates the rotation keys' x and y only (not z), `RemoveRedundantMaterials` hashes a material with SuperFastHash over the property bytes including the terminating NUL of strings, and `fast_atoreal_move` is not `strtof`: the 15-digit fraction scaled by a double table (`native/assimp/fast_atof.rs`).
- `ImportedSkeleton` treats any scene without a node `Dummy01` as a vehicle (`bVehicle`), even when `VehicleSkeleton` is missing too: 13 game files (root `$dummy_root`) take the vehicle key-frame path (wrap-around interpolation, spherical rotation with `Math.acos`/`Math.sin`), and it matches. The `.fbx` rotate modifier path (`Quaternion.mulInverse`, `HelperFunctions.transform` normalising the modifier in place, the default `RootNode` track) and the `skinnedTo` path (`boneIndices.putAll`, hierarchy `addAll`) never occur with a `.x` file, so the probe forces them on the `.x` scenes (`:fbx`, `:skin=<file>` passes) and the port matches those too.
- JOML `Vector3f.min/max` is `a < b ? a : b` (the second operand for NaN, and for -0.0 against 0.0), not `Math.min`; `AiMesh.getTexCoordV` throws for a set with fewer than two components and indexes the buffer with `numUVComponents` per vertex.
- `Float.toString`/`Double.toString` take the EVEN digit on an exact tie between two shortest decimals (JDK 19+); Rust's `{:e}` rounds up. `java::float_to_string` / `double_to_string` were wrong for 9 animation files until `java_even_tie` (test `to_string_takes_the_even_digit_on_an_exact_tie`).
- FBX (E63), measured on the macOS x86-64 library and reproduced on all 2032 files: a model's transform is split into chain nodes `name_$AssimpFbx$_<Component>` (the components that differ from their default, in assimp's order: translation, offsets, pivots, `PreRotation`, `Rotation`, `PostRotation`, scaling, geometric parts); an animated model whose chain has no pivot, offset or geometric part takes the simple path (one channel with the combined keys: `T * R * S` of the interpolated components, decomposed again by `aiMatrix4x4::Decompose`), any other chain a channel per animated component. The simple path is compiled arithmetic that decides the last bit: a column length is `sqrt((x*x + y*y) + z*z)` (the decompiler lists `z` first because it is loaded first, the order of the additions is x, y, z), normalised by a reciprocal multiply; the scale is negated when `!(0 <= det)`; the quaternion comes out of the matrix by the `t > 0` branch with `s = sqrt(1 + t) * 2`, `w = 0.25 * s`. Rotation keys are interpolated with the shortest-path flip `(w*wp) + ((z*zp) + ((x*xp) + (y*yp))) < 0` and the Euler matrix goes through `___sincosf_stret` with the product `Z * Y * X` summed as `((a4*t12 + a3*t8) + a1*t0) + a2*t4`; the order of the length sum was found by feeding 3001 random rotation curves to `door1.fbx` (87 keys were one ulp off with the `z, x, y` order, none with `x, y, z`; `Mat4::decompose` in `scene.rs` is shared with the `.x` paths and `xscene`, `xmodels`, `animclips` stayed `OK`). A bone offset is the cluster's `Transform`; `ColorAndAlpha` properties, `Bool` properties (added as an `int` 0/1, not as one byte) and `Int` are read; the shading model string is lower-cased before it picks the `Material.FbxSurfacePhong`/`Lambert` template and the `$mat.shadingm` property (the file says `Phong` in `Cigar_Lit.fbx` and 13 more); the texture table of `SetTextureProperties` has 40 entries in the library's order (`SpecularFactor` is a specular texture, `ReflectionFactor` a metalness one, then the `Maya|*`, `3dsMax|Parameters|*` and `3dsMax|main|*` PBR names, and the `useGlossiness` branch for roughness and glossiness maps).
- `TriangulateProcess` of this library (E63): a polygon with 4 vertices is a fan that starts at the concave vertex (the sum of two `acosf` angles above `3.1415927f` in `float`), 5 or more are ear-clipped on the projection that drops the axis of the largest Newell-normal component, with the area tests in `double` (`9.999999974752427e-07`); a NaN dot product (two equal points) does not reject an ear (a pentagon of `Bob_BoilerSuit_Ground.fbx` has two vertices at the same position); when an output triangle starts with the vertex the previous one started with it is rotated (the NGON encoding: `(i2, i0, i1)`; the second triangle of a quad `(j1, j2, j0)`); "Failed to triangulate polygon" keeps the triangles found so far. Primitive types become `(p & ~0x1c) | 0x14`. All 2032 files (15 distinct polygon sizes) match.
- `ImportedSkeleton` of `ProcessedAiScene` skips a bone whose `getOffsetMatrix` is null: jassimp sets no offset matrix on a bone without weights (3 vehicle files: `SportsCarWithDoors`, `ModernCarWithDoors_Martin`, `ModernCarWithDoors_ez`). The scene probe prints `offset=null` for it.
- Cameras and lights: three files (`door_pair_left`, `door_pair_right`, `Trailer`) have one camera and one light; the counts and their nodes are compared, their parameters are not dumped (the game's Java never reads them).

Ported: `crates/zomboid-engine/src/native/assimp/` (`scene.rs`, `fast_atof.rs`, `x_file_parser.rs`, `x_file_importer.rs`, `std_sort.rs`, `post_process/{scene_preprocessor,make_left_handed,flip_winding_order,remove_redundant_mats,join_vertices,limit_bone_weights,find_instances,optimize_meshes,triangulate}.rs`, `fbx/{parser,inflate,properties,document,converter}.rs`, `gltf2/{json,asset,importer,libcxx_map}.rs`), `zombie/core/skinnedmodel/{model,animation}/` (`model/jassimp/{processed_ai_scene,imported_skeleton,imported_skinned_mesh,imported_static_mesh,jassimp_importer}.rs`), `org/lwjgl/util/vector/`. Probes: `tests/original_probe/TxtModelsProbe.java`, `AiSceneProbe.java`, `ProcessedSceneProbe.java`. `pz-dump`: arms `txtmodels`, `xscene`, `animscene`, `xmodelscene`, `xmodels`, `animclips`, `fbxscene`, `fbxmodelscene`, `fbxanimscene`, `fbxmodels`, `fbxanims`, `fbxdoc` (scratch: the FBX container), `glbscene`, `glbmodelscene`, `glbanimscene`, `glbmodels`, `glbanims` (`bin/pz-dump-models/mod.rs`). `AiSceneProbe` takes a `+mat` suffix on the flags (also print every material property) and `ProcessedSceneProbe` combined variants (`Normal:last+fbx`).

Status: every part of the 3D model and animation-clip loading that is pure Java is ported and compared (`.txt` models and animations, and the scene processing after the importer), and so are the native `.x`, `.fbx` and glTF2 importers with every post-process step the game asks for (the `.fbx` cases above print `OK` on all 2032 files and the `.glb` cases on all 59; the 3 files with a zero-weight bone are in them, with `offset=null`). No native importer the game's files need is left: what remains native-only is code no game file reaches (the binary/compressed `.x` readers, and the glTF2 features listed in `glTF2: not ported (E66)`: sparse accessors, extensions, morph targets, non-triangle primitives, external buffers). Not ported on the Java side: `ProcessedAiScene.applyToMesh`, `applyToPhysicsShape` and `applyToAnimation` (they copy the compared results into `ModelMesh`, `PhysicsShape` and `AnimationAsset`, the GL and asset-manager objects; the GL upload is skipped by `ModelManager.noOpenGL` anyway), and `JAssImpImporter.replaceHashMapKeys`/`getSharedString` (they only share equal strings: the values are unchanged).

FBX: not ported (E63). No game file reaches any of these (every case above is byte-identical on all 2032 files, nothing is skipped), and each returns an `UNPORTED` error instead of a guess: `LayeredTexture` objects, a texture whose video object carries embedded content (binary or text), a texture with a `UVSet` name outside a mesh, a scene where `FindInstances` would replace a mesh, `MergeMeshes` of skinned meshes in `OptimizeMeshes`, `std::sort` of 24 or more bone weights. Ignored without an error (the library converts them, the game's Java never reads them and the probe does not dump them): camera and light parameters (3 files have one of each; the count and the nodes are compared), `Shape` (blend shape) and `Line` geometry, `RotationOrder` `SphericXYZ` (the library warns too), FBX files older than 2011 (rejected like the library). Materials are dumped by `fbxscene` (`+mat`) and match, although the game's Java never reads an `AiMaterial`.

FBX: measured on the macOS library, UNKNOWN for Windows (E63). The reference is the x86-64 slice of the macOS `libjassimp.dylib` (libc++, Apple libm). The importer itself uses ordered containers and sorts only integer key times (equal keys are indistinguishable), so the STL decision is the one already open for `JoinIdenticalVertices` (`std::unordered_map` order) and `LimitBoneWeights` (`std::sort` ties): MSVC's STL could differ there, UNKNOWN. The libm is the other open point: the rotation keys call `sinf`/`cosf` through Apple's `__sincosf_stret` (not correctly rounded for about 0.026% of arguments, measured), the quad test of `TriangulateProcess` calls `acosf`; this port uses correctly rounded `f64` `sin`/`cos` rounded to `f32`, which matches on every corpus file but differs from the library on a random rotation key about 0.1-0.5% of the time (3001 random rotation curves fed to `door1.fbx`). A Windows build of the library (UCRT `sinf`/`cosf`, MSVC STL) may therefore differ from this reference in the last bit of some rotation keys: UNKNOWN, not measured (no Windows build here).

glTF2: not ported (E66). No game file reaches any of these (every glb case is byte-identical on all 59 files), and each returns an error that starts with `UNPORTED glTF2:` instead of a guess: `extensionsUsed`/`extensionsRequired` and any extension object on a material, mesh, primitive, skin, node or animation (the 59 files use none), sparse accessors, buffers with a `uri` or a second buffer, images given as a data URI, morph targets and mesh `weights`, cameras and lights on a node, `skin.skeleton`, a skin without `inverseBindMatrices`, material members other than `name`, `pbrMetallicRoughness` (`baseColorFactor`, `baseColorTexture`, `metallicFactor`, `roughnessFactor`), `emissiveFactor`, `doubleSided`, `alphaMode`, `alphaCutoff` (so no normal, occlusion, emissive or metallic-roughness texture), texture info members other than `index` and `texCoord`, an animation channel without a node, primitive modes other than TRIANGLES (the library decodes POINTS, LINES, LINE_LOOP, LINE_STRIP, TRIANGLE_STRIP and TRIANGLE_FAN; it is decompiled but not ported), colour accessors that are not VEC4, weights that are not float VEC4, joints that are not u8/u16 VEC4, an inverse bind matrix or keyframe accessor that is not float. glTF 1 (`version 1` in the GLB header) is another importer (`glTFImporter`) and `.gltf` text files are not game files (all 59 are binary `.glb`, version 2); neither is ported. Not visible through jassimp and so not dumped: the embedded texture bytes, scene and node metadata, the asset block.

glTF2 (E66), measured on the macOS x86-64 library and reproduced on all 59 files and on six hand-made files: the objects are loaded lazily (`LazyDict::Retrieve`): an object is created on its first use and appended to its dictionary AFTER its own `Read`, so a node's children (and their meshes) come before the node and a mesh before the skin that uses it; `Asset::Load` retrieves the default scene's nodes (the `scene` member, else `scenes[0]`), then every skin, then every animation (a channel target outside the scene loads that node). The `Ref` index is the load-order position, and it decides the order of the `aiMesh`es, the material indices (a primitive without a material gets the index after the loaded ones, a default material appended), the numbering of embedded textures (`*N`) and the keys of the channel map; objects nothing refers to are never loaded. An unnamed node is named after its id (`nodes[N]`), so bone names are never invented. A mesh gives one `aiMesh` per primitive (`<name>-<p>` when there are several), vertices are renumbered by first use in the index buffer, normals, colours and UV sets are read only when their accessor has the position count, tangents only next to normals (bitangent `(N ^ T) * w`, the cross product times the tangent's w), colours as `u8/255.0f`, `u16/65535.0f` or float, `V = 1 - v`; a face with an index out of range is dropped. Bone weights come from every `JOINTS_n/WEIGHTS_n` set (weight above 0, joint below the joint count), a bone without weights gets one weight `(0, 0.0)`. Animations: the channel order is that of a libc++ `std::unordered_map<unsigned, ...>` keyed by the node's `Ref` index (identity hash, bucket counts 2, 5, 11, 23, 47, 97, a new node first in its bucket, rehash behind the bucket head: `libcxx_map.rs`, 120-channel files and shuffled trees match), key times are `float(t) * 1000.0f`, `CUBICSPLINE` takes the value at offset 1 of 3, `STEP` is read as linear, a node without a channel path but with static TRS gets a single key at time 0, the duration is the largest last key and the ticks per second 1000. Materials: properties in the library's order (`?mat.name`, `$clr.diffuse`, `$clr.base`, textures with `$tex.file` (`*N` for an embedded image, else the uri), `$tex.uvwsrc`, then with a sampler `$tex.mappingname`, `$tex.mappingid`, `$tex.mapmodeu/v` (wrap 0, clamp 1, mirror 2) and the raw GL filter enums when set; WITHOUT a sampler only `$tex.mapmodeu/v` = 0), `$mat.metallicFactor`, `$mat.roughnessFactor`, `$mat.shininess`, `$clr.emissive`, `$mat.twosided`, `$mat.opacity`, `$mat.gltf.alphaMode`, `$mat.gltf.alphaCutoff`, `$mat.shadingm` = 11). Numbers are `rapidjson`'s: an integer stays integer, a fraction is `d / 10^k` in double with at most 17 significant digits (not `strtod`), converted to float. An image without a `uri` needs `bufferView` and `mimeType` (the library's error text is reproduced).

glTF2: measured on the macOS library, UNKNOWN for Windows (E66). The reference is the x86-64 slice of the macOS `libjassimp.dylib`. Two places depend on the standard library or libm: the channel order of an animation comes from libc++'s `std::unordered_map` (MSVC's STL has another bucket and node order, so on Windows the `aiAnimation` channels, and with them the order the game sees bones animated, could differ; UNKNOWN) and the rotation matrix of a node given as a TRS quaternion uses float arithmetic of the compiled library (port: the same float operations in the same order, matches on all 59 files and on the synthetic ones; whether MSVC contracts them differently is UNKNOWN). The importer reads no `sinf`/`cosf`.

## Radio data and scripts by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh radio` -> `OK   radio: 75917 lines identical`. The booted original server (`RadioProbe` in `ServerHarness`) runs 718 commands of `tests/radio_cases.txt` on the ORIGINAL classes (`ZomboidRadio`, `RadioScriptManager`, `RadioChannel`, `RadioScript`, `RadioBroadCast`, `RadioLine`, `RadioData`, `RadioTranslationData`, `Translator`, `Rand`) and `pz-dump radio` runs the same commands on the port; every step prints the state both sides reached. Inputs: the player's own `radio/data/RADIO_SAVE.txt` (`radiosave @real`), 28 hand-made save files, files the original's own `Save` wrote (`saveto`/`updatescripts ... name`, then `radiosave @name`), 87 crafted `RadioData` XML files (88 `xml` commands) and 21 translation files in `tests/radio_crafted` (invented IDs and texts; nothing of the game's data is committed). `RadioData.xml` of the game (the only radio file; 1.5 MB: 18 channels, 36 scripts, 1123 broadcasts, 13166 lines) is read by the port itself from `media/radio` and its texts from the `RD_<id>` translations of `RadioData.json`.

| What | Original | Status | Case lines |
|---|---|---|---|
| Channels, scripts, broadcasts, lines of the real `RadioData.xml` with the random adverts | `RadioData.fetchAllRadioData`, `ReadFile`, `loadRootInfo`, `loadRadioScripts`, `loadAdverts`, `loadChannels`, `loadScripts`, `loadBroadcast` (the `Rand.Next(101) < 75`, `< 50` and `Next(size)` draws for the pre or post segment), `loadExitOptions` | compared: every field of every channel, script, broadcast and line (colours and air time as float bits, text, codes, the segment links) and the Rand draw count (1529 draws for the default start), after a new game and after a loaded game | `fulldump` (18232 lines for the default start) and `state` (81 of them) |
| Crafted XML: 87 files | the same, with the exceptions the original logs | compared: not XML, empty, no `RadioData`, no `RootInfo`, bad/negative/space/plus/big versions, GUID twice, no channels, bad or lower-case or empty categories, a missing attribute at each level (the helpful `NullPointerException` text is the same), bad numbers (frequency `9x`, `3000000000`, `1000.0`, Arabic-Indic digits, loops, stamps, colours, chances, delays), overlapping/negative/zero-length/descending stamps and segments (which ignore stamps), exit options over 100 or negative, duplicate scripts or broadcasts, adverts (empty, with exit options, a name equal to another's GUID, a missing category), odd nesting, comments, CDATA, entities and a DOCTYPE, BOM, encodings, UTF-16 | `xml` |
| Custom air time in a line's text `${t:..}` and what the channel airs | `checkForCustomAirTimer`, `RadioChannel.update`, `UpdateScripts`, `SetPlayerIsListening`, `getNextScript` (loops, exit options with `Rand.Next(100)`, delays) | compared: 48 texts (`2.5`, `abc`, an upper-case tag, `0`, `-1`, `1e2`, padded, `NaN`, `Infinity`, `1.5f`, `1d`, 28 hex floats, empty, two tags, unclosed, a tag that is not at the start), and 38 `xchan` steps over the crafted scripts (loops 2..5, min > max, exit chances 40/30/40, delays) | `x_cdt.xml`, `x_basic.xml` |
| `ZomboidRadio.Init` for a new game | `Init(-1)`: channels from the XML, `ObfuscateChannelCheck`, the Lua handlers of `OnLoadRadioScripts`, `timeSinceApo` months * 30.5F, `simulateScriptsUntil(days, true)`, `checkGameModeSpecificStart` ("Initial Infection": `init_infection` with an exit option back to the current script, a start day stamp of days+1 for the others; "Six Months Later": `numbers` and `pastor`) | compared: time since the apocalypse 1, 2, 4, 7, 13, three game modes, the dynamic channel frequency drawn (`dynamic reset`) or restored (`dynamic saved 100400/93200/5`) | `init -1` |
| `ZomboidRadio.Init` for a loaded game, `Load` | `Init(v)`, `Load`, `RadioScriptManager.Load`, `RadioChannel.setActiveScript` with loop and maxloops, `LoadAiringBroadcast`, `setTimeSynced`, `simulateScriptsUntil(days, false)` | compared (73 `init` commands): no file; the player's real file (CRLF), also with 5 months and 3 or 9 nights behind the world; 28 hand-made files (spaces and tabs, blank lines, unknown channels, 6 and 8 fields, unknown scripts, `none` in any case, negative and huge numbers, `+1`/`01` forms, bad numbers at every field, `DaysSinceStart` without `=`, with two `=`, repeated, with a prefix, `LvObfuscation` empty/yes/TRUE, CR, CRLF, no final newline, a byte order mark, bad UTF-8, an empty file, duplicate channel lines); three files `Save` wrote; the same files over a world with a day count, to see which ones fall back to the options | `init 100` |
| `UpdateScripts`, `Save`, the dedupe of an unchanged file | `ZomboidRadio.UpdateScripts` (the day counter at hour 0), `Save`, `RadioScriptManager.Save` | compared: the text of the file (`DaysSinceStart`, `LvObfuscation`, one line per channel in insertion order) after 8:00, 8:01, midnight, 5:59, 12:00, 23:45 and so on, and whether the file is rewritten | `updatescripts`, `saveto` |
| `ZomboidRadio.update` and the channel tick | `update`, `RadioScriptManager.update`, `RadioChannel.update` (`airCounter -= 1.25F * GameTime.getMultiplier()`, the line text, `minmod`/`maxmod`, `airCounterMultiplier`), `SendTransmission`, `applyWeatherInterference`, `scrambleString`, the obfuscation of a channel, `disableBroadcasting`, `postRadioSilence` after 14 days | compared: 27144 tick lines (counter bits, line number, last aired line) and the Rand draw count of every sending; multipliers 0.5, 1, 5, 40; weather interference 0 (not running, or strength 0), 0.00035, 0.175 (rain 0.5, tropical storm 0.25), 0.63 (blizzard 0.9), 0.7 (thunderstorm 1.0), 1.05 (rain 3.0); flags `disable`/`louisville`; days 0, 14, 15, 30 | `tick`, `listen`, `weather`, `flags` |
| Name tables | `addChannelName`, `removeChannelName`, `getRandomFrequency` (`Rand.Next(min, max) / 200 * 200`, retried while known), `knownFrequencies`, `categorizedChannels` | compared | `addname`, `removename`, `randfreq` |
| Text effects | `scrambleString` (percent <= 0, >= 100, with and without BBCode, custom scramble, Java whitespace including U+001C, U+000B, U+2028, not U+00A0), `getRandomBzztFzzt`, `computerize` (`Character.isLetter`, simple `toLowerCase`/`toUpperCase`) | compared: 28 scrambles, 20 bzzt, 7 computerize (Latin, Greek, Cyrillic, Arabic, Hebrew, Thai, CJK, special case mappings); the char tables themselves for all 65536 UTF-16 units (377 letter ranges, 370 digits, 2375 case mappings) | `scramble`, `bzzt`, `computerize`, `charmap` |
| `RadioTranslationData` (`ReadFile`, `loadTranslations`, `validate`) | the same | compared: 21 files (`[Info]`, `[Translations]`, `[Collection]` with members, unclosed, before open, missing fields, bad versions, unknown and other languages, `=` inside values, CR and CRLF, no-break spaces, bad UTF-8, a byte order mark, missing file) | `trans` |

`sh tools/oracle.sh radio` and `cargo test --release -p zomboid-engine --test oracle_hashes radio_matches_original` print `OK`; `moddata` (1338), `sounds` (160014), `javachars`, `luastring`, `translator`, `erosionini` and `scriptparser` stay `OK` after the changes to `java.rs`.

Measured (each a line of the oracle, the port reproduces it):
- `ZomboidRadio.Load()` returns TRUE after a file whose lines all loaded. The decompiled text (`return result; ... finally { result = true; }`) says false and misled the first port; the bytecode (`javap -c`: `RadioScriptManager.Load`, `iconst_1; istore_1`, `iload_1; ireturn`) and the oracle say true, so `Init` keeps the file's `DaysSinceStart` (30, 4, 7, 8, even -5) and `LvObfuscation` and simulates only the channels the file did not mark synced. Any exception while reading (a bad `DaysSinceStart`, a `DaysSinceStart` or `LvObfuscation` line without `=`, a bad number in a channel line) returns false and `Init` recomputes `(int)((timeSinceApo-1) * 30.5F) + nights survived`; the channel lines before the bad one stay applied, the others have no script.
- `Load` clears every channel's script first, marks every channel named in a line synced even when the line has not 7 fields, ignores a line for a channel that is not registered, ignores an unknown script name, and reads the day and the line only of a script/broadcast that is not `none`; `LoadAiringBroadcast` with a negative line only stores `lastBroadcastId`.
- `LvObfuscation = yes` is false (`Boolean.parseBoolean`, any case of `true` is true); `ZomboidRadio.louisvilleObfuscation` (like `disableBroadcasting`) is static and survives `Reset()`, so a later `init` sees the value of the earlier one (the oracle's `init` copies it; `flags` sets it explicitly where it matters); `Init` sets `postRadioSilence` to false first.
- Every vanilla `main` script has loops 1-1 and no exit option (`init_infection` gets one from `checkGameModeSpecificStart`); its last broadcast ends between stamp 1410 (95000, one day) and 40110 (204, about 28 days), after which `UpdateScripts` makes the current script `none` (`getNextScript` finds no exit option; a `Rand.Next(100)` is still drawn). A new game with 5 months behind it (`days=152`) has no script left at its first `UpdateScripts`. `simulateScriptsUntil` calls `UpdateScripts(currentTimeStamp, i*24*60)` with the time stamp of the manager (0), so it does not end a script that has a broadcast.
- `RadioChannel.setActiveScript` draws `Rand.Next(min, max)` for the number of loops only when the maximum is -1 and `min != max && min <= max`; `AddExitOption` refuses an option that takes the total above 100 (negative chances count), `RadioScript.AddBroadcast` refuses stamps that start before the previous end, are negative or end at or before the start unless the broadcast is a segment.
- `RadioData` takes attributes with `getNamedItem(name).getTextContent()` and `trim`: a missing attribute is the helpful `NullPointerException` and ends the load, what was read before stays (channels in `getRadioChannels`). `Integer.parseInt` takes `+5`, `-5`, Arabic-Indic and other BMP digits (`java::parse_int` now does), rejects spaces and `1000.0`.
- The XML parser is Xerces: a byte order mark or the first bytes choose UTF-8 or UTF-16, then the `encoding` of the declaration (ISO-8859-1, ISO-8859-15, windows-1252, US-ASCII work; a UTF-8 BOM before ISO-8859-1 is skipped; a UTF-16 stream declaring UTF-8 fails; an unknown name, bytes that are not valid in the encoding, `version="2.0"`/`"1.2"`, a control character, two roots or text after the root fail; `"1.1"` is fine; entities of an internal DTD expand; `&nbsp;` is an error). The port decodes the same way (`decode_xml`) and reads the result with `pz_xml_util`.
- Only a text that starts with `${t:` (after `trim`, any case) is looked at, and the pattern `\$\{t:([^}]+)\}` is case-sensitive, so `${T:4}` is left in the text. The number is lower-cased and trimmed before `Float.parseFloat`: `NaN` and `Infinity` become `nan` and `infinity` and fail, `1e2`, `1.5f`, `1d`, padded numbers and hex floats parse. A failed parse removes the tag and leaves the air time -1; a parsed value is the line's air time and the text gets the prefix `[cdt=<Float.toString>]` only when it is >= 0 (`java::parse_float` reads hex floats now: one rounding, ties to even, compared on 28 literals).

Ported: `zombie.radio.ZomboidRadio` (`Init`, `Load`, `Save`, `UpdateScripts`, `update`, `PlayerListensChannel`, `SendTransmission` without devices, `scrambleString`, `computerize`, `getRandomBzztFzzt`, `getRandomFrequency`, the name tables, `ObfuscateChannelCheck`, `checkGameModeSpecificStart`), `RadioScriptManager`, `RadioChannel` (with `DynamicRadioChannel`), `RadioScript` (with `ExitOption`), `RadioBroadCast`, `RadioLine`, `RadioData` (`fetchAllRadioData` for the vanilla folder, `ReadFile`, every `load*`), `RadioTranslationData`, `ChannelCategory`, the time stamp arithmetic of `RadioAPI`; in `java.rs`: `parse_int` digits of every script, hex floats, the simple case maps; in `java_chars.rs`: `is_letter`, `digit10`, `to_lower_char`, `to_upper_char`; `Translator::radio_data_mut`.

The Lua side is stood in for, and proved only for the game's Lua: of the `OnLoadRadioScripts` handlers only `media/lua/server/radio/ISDynamicRadio.lua` touches the radio (it registers the `DynamicRadioChannel` "Automated Emergency Broadcast System", uuid `EMRG-711984`, category Emergency, a frequency range 88000..108000 drawn with `getRandomFrequency` on the first run and then kept in `getGameTime():getModData().dynamicRadio`, air counter multiplier 1.0, `AddChannel(channel, false)`); `ISWeatherChannel.lua`'s handler only calls `WeatherChannel.Init()`. `pz-dump radio` passes a Rust closure with that behaviour as `on_load_radio_scripts`; the probe resets the Lua state per scenario by reflection, so the five scenarios (`reset`, 100400, 93200 taken, 5 out of range, none) compare the real Lua with the closure. A mod's handler is not covered.

UNKNOWN / not ported:
- Single player and client: the harness is a dedicated server (`GameServer.server`), so `getGameMode()` is `Server`; the `SinglePlayer` and `Client` branches (device wake-up in `UpdateScripts`, the player-speech loop of `update`, `GameClient.sendRadioServerDataRequest`, `LouisvilleObfuscationCheck`, which is a no-op on a server) are not compared.
- Devices: `DeviceData` and `WaveSignalDevice` lookups (`RegisterDevice`, `DistributeTransmission`, `DistributeToPlayer`, `doDeviceRangeDistortion`) are not ported (the server has no device; the oracle sees a transmission only through the Rand draws of its scrambling), nor `RecordedMedia.init`/`save`, `StorySoundManager`, `FreqListEntry`, and the network (`GameServer.sendIsoWaveSignal` and `sendRadioPostSilence` loop over no connection; `WriteRadioServerDataPacket`).
- Mods (`RadioData.fetchRadioData` over mod folders), the community translation files of `loadRadioScripts` (language other than EN with content translations enabled; `currentTranslation` is read by `checkForTranslation`, which nothing calls, so the output would not change), `getTranslatorNames` and the order of several `media/radio/*.xml` files (the port sorts the names; the game has one file, so `File.list` order is unobserved).
- XML decoding: only the encodings listed above; any other JDK charset name (the original accepts many) fails in the port, UNKNOWN; XML features beyond those in the crafted files (external entities, namespaces) are unmeasured.
- The Unicode tables of `Character.isLetter` come from the JDK that runs the oracle here (`java` 27.0.1); the game's own JRE (25) may differ for characters added in Unicode 17 (UNKNOWN). `String.toLowerCase`/`toUpperCase` (full mappings) are untouched.
- The probe sets weather interference through the weather period's fields, the time of day, nights and the multiplier through `GameTime`'s fields; `GameTime.getMultiplier` is computed in the port for a server that is not fast-forwarding (`multiplier * fps * bias * perObject * slomo * 0.8F`, compared on 7 values).

## Save files by direct port (2026-11-16)
E53 measured which files the original game writes into a save folder and which of them its loaders read, against what was already compared, then ported the loaders that have decompiled Java and proved each against the ORIGINAL loader inside the booted server (`ServerHarness`: a headless dedicated server on the pinned jar, a fresh scratch cache and save folder; the probe copies the player's real file in, or writes one with the ORIGINAL writer, calls the original loader and prints the state it leaves; `pz-dump` prints the same from the port). The real data is the isolated profile's save (`profile/Saves/Sandbox/2026-11-07_22-34-13`, world version 249). Every hand-made or writer-made input is committed under `tests/<format>_crafted/` (tiny, none is game data) and read by the Rust side by file name. Loaders swallow exceptions (`ExceptionLogger`), so what is compared is the state left after a failure too: a table, a symbol list or a chunk list keeps what was read before the failing element.

| Format (file in the save) | Original loader (writer) | Status | Case (`sh tools/oracle.sh <case>`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Global object systems `gos_<name>.bin` (5 real files: farming 10464 bytes, campfire, feedingTrough, rainbarrel, trap) | `SGlobalObjectSystem.load` (`save`), `KahluaTable.load`, `SGlobalObjects` lookup | compared: the real files, a file the original writer makes (tables of every type, NaN and infinities, unicode, extreme and out-of-map coordinates), hand-made headers and payloads, the writer file cut at every length | `gos` (1186 lines) |
| `map_worldgen.bin` | `WorldGenParams.load` (`save`) | compared (real, writer, hand-made, every cut) | `savemeta` (873 lines) |
| `map_symbols.bin` | `MapItem.LoadWorldMap` / `WorldMapSymbols.load` (`SaveWorldMap`) | compared | `savemeta` |
| `statistics.bin` | `StatisticsManager.load` (`save`), with the achievements the values unlock | compared | `savemeta` |
| `metadata.bin` | `MetaTracker.load` body: `Helicopter.load`, `AmbientStreamManager.load` (`save`); the class's own `load()` runs on the real file | compared (the dedicated server's helicopter branch; the single-player branch of `Helicopter.load` is not ported) | `savemeta` |
| `map_visited.bin` (4,016,044 bytes) | `WorldMapVisited.getInstance` load, legacy format included (`save`) | compared: real file, files made by the original's own mutators and `save()` (the port re-applies the same commands and writes the same hash), hand-made headers, short and long bodies, `MapAllKnown` | `visited` (80 lines) |
| `isoregiondata/datachunk_*.bin`, `RegionHeader.bin` (40 chunks) | `IsoRegionWorker.loadSaveMap`, `JobChunkUpdate.addChunkFromFile`/`readChunksPacket`, `DataChunk.load`, `DataRoot` lookups (`JobChunkUpdate.saveChunksToDisk`) | compared (the saved flags); the regions derived from them are not ported (below) | `isoregion` (151 lines) |
| `erosion.ini` | `ErosionConfig.readFile` (`writeFile`) | compared: real file and 41 hand-made files | `erosionini` (3655 lines) |
| `map_ver.bin`, the `GMTM` header of `map_t.bin` | `IsoWorld.readWorldVersion`, the `map_ver.bin` read of `IsoWorld.init`, `GameWindow.ReadString(DataInputStream)` (`GameWindow.save`) | compared | `mapver` (40 lines) |
| `map_t.bin` body (GameTime), `map_sand.bin`, `map_meta.bin`, `map_zone.bin`, `map_basements.bin`, `metagrid/*`, `map_animals.bin`, `z_outfits.bin`, `global_mod_data.bin`, `iTrack.bin`, `fishingData.bin`, `recorded_media.bin`, `WorldDictionary.bin`, `map/<x>/<y>` chunk files, `vehicles.db`, `players.db` | | compared before E53 (the sections below: `gametime`, `sandbox`, `mapmeta`, `mapzone`, `animalzones`, `outfits`, `moddata`, `FishingSaveProbe`, `chunksave`, `worlddictionary`) | those cases |
| `zpop/zpop_*.bin`, `zpop/zpop_ids.bin`, `zpop/zpop_virtual.bin`, `chunkdata/chunkdata_*.bin` | native code (`PZPopMan64`, `MapCollisionData`) | NOT compared, no Java loader exists (below) | none |
| `reanimated.bin` (8 bytes: version 249, 0 zombies) | `ReanimatedPlayers.loadReanimatedPlayers` | NOT compared: it reads `IsoObject.factoryFromFileInput` and `IsoZombie.load`, and `IsoZombie` is not ported; the real file holds no zombie | none |
| `entity_data.bin` (4 bytes: 0 entities) | `GameEntityManager.load` (`MetaEntitySystem.saveMetaEntities`) | NOT compared: needs the entity engine and component loaders; the real file holds no entity | none |
| `radio/data/RADIO_SAVE.txt` (649 bytes, CRLF, 19 channels) | `ZomboidRadio.Load` (`Save`) | compared by E56 (section `Radio data and scripts by direct port`): the real file, 28 hand-made files and files the original's own `Save` wrote, with the channels that `RadioData.xml` creates | `radio` |
| `map.bin` (12 bytes: `IsoCell.save`'s width, height, max height) | written by `GameWindow.save`; no loader in the game (only the legacy `WorldConverter` reads such a file) | nothing to compare | none |
| `mods.txt` | `ActiveModsFile` read/write (E50, section below) | not re-compared here | none |
| `id_manager_data.bin`, `important_area_data.bin` | inline in `IsoWorld.init` (`ObjectIDManager.load`, ...) | NOT compared: not in this save, so there is no real data, and `init` cannot run on the server | none |
| `thumb.png`, `WorldDictionaryLog.lua`, `WorldDictionaryReadable.lua`, `players.db-journal`, `apop/` (empty) | written outputs, never read back by the game | nothing to compare | none |

`sh tools/oracle.sh gos savemeta visited isoregion erosionini mapver` all print `OK`; `chunksave` (90883), `mapmeta` (4926), `moddata` (1338) and `sounds` (160014) stay `OK`. `cargo test -p zomboid-engine --test oracle_hashes` checks the six new sha256 files without Java.

Measured quirks (each is a line of an oracle, and the port reproduces it):
- File loaders read the file into the shared 10 MiB `SliceY.SliceBuffer` and `limit(n)` it. A `GameWindow.ReadString(ByteBuffer)` whose count runs past the limit reads whatever the buffer held beyond the data, so a truncated `map_symbols.bin` symbol string or `gos_*.bin` payload gives a result that depends on the buffer's earlier use. The probes zero the buffer before such loads and the port models it as zeros (`ByteBuffer::wrap_slice_buffer`); a count that passes the buffer's capacity is the original's `IllegalArgumentException: newLimit > capacity: (P > C)` (`StringUTF.load`'s `limit()` call). UNKNOWN: what a real session's stale bytes would be (the live game never starts a load on a zeroed buffer).
- `KahluaTable.load` empties the table first and keeps every entry read before a failure; `WorldMapSymbols.load` appends one symbol at a time and keeps the earlier ones when a later one fails (a bad type or texture string ends the list there).
- `WorldMapVisited`: flags passed to `setFlags`/`clearFlags` are not masked and the unit arithmetic is on signed bytes, so a flag value with bits above the unit's two spills into the higher units of the same byte (or is cut off at the byte's end) exactly as in the original; the legacy format's rows are read through one buffer, so a short row keeps the previous row's tail.
- `IsoRegions`: `DataChunk.load` activates levels from `min(highestZ, 31)` down to 0 (`ensureSquares` ignores 32 and above) but keeps the file's `highestZ` (`hz=32` and `hz=40` print as read); a byte count that is smaller or larger than the flag array fails only past the end of the array (the bytes before it are kept). A chunk file whose length field is 3000 or -4 stops the whole load after the discovery list got its id (`disc=[...]`, no chunk). A header that names chunks without a file makes the original warn and throw `IsoRegionException` ("previously discovered chunks have not been loaded") after the files were queued, and the loaded chunks stay.
- `ErosionConfig.readFile` splits each line with `split("=")`: `a = ` gives two pieces (`"a "` and `" "`), so an empty value fails `Integer.parseInt` (the read stops with false and keeps what was read before), while `a =` gives one piece and is only logged as unknown; `a = 5=` is a valid line (trailing empty pieces are dropped), `a = 1 = 2` is unknown. `debug.enabled` is `Boolean.parseBoolean` (`tRuE` is true, `yes` is false); a UTF-8 BOM in front of a key is not removed by `trim()`, so that key is unknown; invalid UTF-8 inside a value fails the number.
- `IsoWorld.readWorldVersion` reads `map_t.bin`'s `GMTM` magic on a server and `map_ver.bin`'s first int otherwise (-1 for a missing, short or foreign file); `GameWindow.ReadString(DataInputStream)` returns `""` for length 0, throws `RuntimeException` over 65536 and `NegativeArraySizeException` for a negative length (`new StringBuilder(len)`), then reads that many UTF-16 chars.

How the probes get the cases the real save does not have: the gos, savemeta and isoregion writers are the ORIGINAL classes (`SGlobalObjectSystem.save`, `WorldGenParams.save`, `MapItem.SaveWorldMap`, `StatisticsManager.save`, `Helicopter.save`, `JobChunkUpdate.addChunkFromDataChunk` + `saveChunksToDisk`), and `map_visited.bin` is made by the original's own `save()` (4 MB, so only the commands are committed). Probe-side shortcuts that are not the original's code path: `MapVerProbe` switches `GameServer.server` off for the length of one `readWorldVersion` call and repeats the three lines `IsoWorld.init` runs on `map_ver.bin` (`init` cannot run on a server); `IsoRegionProbe` constructs an `IsoRegionWorker` without starting its thread and runs the queued `ChunkUpdate` jobs through `JobChunkUpdate.readChunksPacket` itself, with the job pool emptied and the worker's static 2076 byte buffer zeroed first (a pooled job keeps the stale bytes of an earlier one); the chunk files are handed to the port in file-name order, while the original lists the directory in file-system order (every multi-file scenario loads all files without a failure, and the discovery list is printed sorted).

Ported: `zombie.globalObjects` (`SGlobalObjects`, `SGlobalObjectSystem` load, `GlobalObjectLookup`), `KahluaTable.load` (`load_into`), `zombie.statistics` (`StatisticsManager`, `Statistic`, `StatisticType`, `StatisticCategory`, `Achievement`), `zombie.iso.MetaTracker` (`Helicopter.load` on the server, `Alarm`, `AmbientStreamManager.load`), `WorldGenParams.load`, `WorldMapSymbols.load` and `MapItem.LoadWorldMap`, `WorldMapVisited` (grid, mutators, `save`, `load` with the legacy format), `zombie.iso.areas.isoregion` (`IsoRegions.hash`, `DataChunk`, `DataRoot` select/get, `JobChunkUpdate`, `IsoRegionWorker.loadSaveMap`), `zombie.erosion.ErosionConfig` (fields, `readFile`, `writeFile`), `IsoWorld.readWorldVersion` and the `map_ver.bin` read (`zombie/iso/world_version.rs`). `ByteBuffer` (`java_nio.rs`) now has a capacity and `GameWindow.ReadString(ByteBuffer)`'s behaviour past the limit.

UNKNOWN / not ported:
- `zpop/*` and `chunkdata/*` are written and read by native libraries (`ZombiePopulationManager` has `private static native` methods `n_init`, `n_config`, `n_updateMain`, `n_save`, ... and loads `PZPopMan64`; `MapCollisionData` has `n_init`, `n_chunkUpdateTask`, `n_save`, ... and only hands the sub-folder names `chunkdata` and `zpop` to the native side with `n_setGameState`). The Java side has no loader to port, so this is outside the direct-port method (porting decompiled Java); the formats are not documented anywhere in the Java, and nothing here was attempted on the native side. The real save's `zpop_32_45.bin` (28,280 bytes), `zpop_virtual.bin` (11,830), `zpop_ids.bin` (6) and `chunkdata_32_45.bin` are therefore unchecked.
- Isoregion data derived from the saved flags (`IsoChunkRegion`, `IsoWorldRegion`, `DataRoot.processDirtyChunks`, flood fill and merging) and the network packet forms of the same data are not ported; the `isoregion` case compares the loaded `DataRoot` (levels, flags, square lookups by world coordinate) only. The writers (`JobChunkUpdate.saveChunksToDisk`, `DataChunk.save`) run from the original in the probe and are not ported (the probe writes the crafted `RegionHeader.bin` itself, in the format the worker thread writes: version, count, ids), so the port reads these saves and cannot write them; the same holds for `gos`, `savemeta` and the `map_ver.bin` writer. The writers that are ported and compared are `WorldMapVisited.save` (equal file hashes) and `ErosionConfig.writeFile`'s text (the file I/O around it is not).
- `Helicopter.load`'s single-player branch, `MetaTracker.save`, `StatisticsManager.save`, `WorldGenParams.save`, `MapItem.SaveWorldMap` and the `gos` writer exist only in the original (they produced the committed inputs).
- (E56 fixed the first part: `java::parse_int` takes the BMP digits of every script and `java::parse_double`/`parse_float` read hex floats, both measured in the `radio` oracle and by unit tests; the E53 cases still print `OK`.)
