A Java string holding an unpaired surrogate (a `map_ver.bin` map list written with one) comes out of the port with U+FFFD, since a Rust `String` cannot hold it (that case was left out of `mapver`).
- The speed scaling `ErosionMain.initConfig` applies after reading `erosion.ini`, `ErosionConfig`'s network `save`/`load` (`ByteBufferWriter`), and everything that happens to these states after the load.

## Zombie AI states by direct port (2026-11-17)
`sh tools/oracle.sh zombieai` -> `OK   zombieai: 36893 lines identical` (E62: 24 scenarios, 18,365 lines; E65a added the animation root movement, collision and `separate`: `zsq` and 16 moving scenarios; about 3 minutes; `tests/oracle_hashes.rs::zombieai_matches_original`); `actioncontext` (143826 lines), `zombie` (2997), `animsets` (91705) and `climate` (306807) stay identical. `ZombieAiProbe` runs the ORIGINAL `zombie.characters.IsoZombie` AI inside the booted dedicated-server harness: one zombie on the square (8280, 11670, 0) (seeded as `zombie`/`actioncontext` do) and a player stand-in at (8280.5 + dx, 11670.5 + dy), ticked as the world ticks its objects (`preupdate(); update(); postupdate();`). Scenario `zai:<seed>:<ticks>:<dx>:<dy>:<force|none>`; `force` sets `setTarget(player)` and `timeSinceSeenFlesh = 0` before every tick, `none` leaves the zombie without a target. Per tick it prints the draws of the global `Rand`, the ActionContext state and children, the AI state (`getCurrentState().getClass().getSimpleName()`), position (`Float.toString`), direction, forward vector, look angle, health, target, the player's health, the path behaviour's goal and 40 animation variables (the ones the states and transitions read, including the `AnimationPlayer` angles and twists, `previousState`, `bStopLunging`, `distanceToTarget`, the ragdoll getters); before the first tick it prints all 164 game variables of the zombie (`VAR name Type value`).

How the original zombie AI runs headless (decided by measurement; the decision for E57/E60 was left open): (1) a standalone state without the server harness was not tried (the zombie needs `SandboxOptions`, the AnimSets, `ScriptManager` and the world's squares the harness already boots). (2) The dedicated-server harness with `GameServer.server = false` from before the zombie is constructed until the scenario ends works and is the recipe: on a dedicated server every `IsoZombie.setVariable` overload is a no-op (E60: `getGameVariables()` = 0, the ActionContext never leaves `idle`), so no transition can fire; with the flag false the zombie registers its 164 variable callbacks (102 Boolean, 35 Float, 24 String, 3 Int; enums are strings), `IsoGameCharacter.updateAnimationTimeDelta`/`postUpdateInternal` take the client paths (the animation updates every 5th tick, so the first animation frame and the first state change are on tick 5), `IsoCell.getGridSquare` goes through an `IsoChunkMap` (built over chunks 1034..1035 x 1458..1459 around the player, `numPlayers` 1) and `IsoZombie.update` needs `ServerLOS.init()` (without it a NullPointerException as soon as a target is set). The player stand-in is an `IsoPlayer` made BEFORE the RNG is seeded (its construction draws are not in the zombie's sequence) with `IsoCamera.setCameraCharacter`, `IsoMovingObject.setIDCount(0)` first (the player is object 1, the zombie object 2: `WalkTowardState` reads `getID()`), and the options and `GameServer.server` restored after each scenario.

Ported (`crates/zomboid-engine/src/zombie/ai/states/`, `zombie/characters/iso_zombie_sp.rs`, `zombie/ai/walking_on_the_spot.rs`; every fn opens with the Java class and method it follows): `ZombieIdleState` extended (the wander test also counts the player's square; `IsoGameCharacter.pathToAux` with `PolygonalMap2.lineClearCollide` sets `bPathfind` or `bMoving` in single-player), `PathFindState` (`enter`/`execute`/`exit`), `LungeState`, `AttackState` (`enter`, `execute`, `exit`, `animEvent` for `SetAttackOutcome`), `ZombieFaceTargetState`, `WalkTowardState` (`enter`, `execute` for a zombie without a target, `exit`), `WalkingOnTheSpot`; the single-player zombie in `iso_zombie_sp.rs` (the 164-slot variable table, read lazily as the original's callback slots are, through `IAnimationVariableSource` views for the ActionContext and the animator; `updateInternal`/`postUpdateInternal`/`postUpdateAnimating` with the order of the draws; `StateMachine.changeRootState`, `StateMachineComponent.actionStateChanged`; `bAttack`, `blunge`, `bstoplunging`, `isFacingTarget`, `distancetotarget`, `turndirection`, the turning flags and `setTurningAround` with the `Turn180Started`/`Turn180TargetChanged` global anim events; the `Footstep` anim event); `AnimationPlayer` angles (`updateForwardDirection`, `setTargetDirection`, `DoAngles` twist, `getLookAngle`; the bones and poses are not ported); `PathFindBehavior2.update` up to the point a path is found; `LineClearCollideMain` (`isNotClearOld`, `canStandAtOld`, `supercover` over the squares); `IsoGridSquare.isDoorTo`/`isSomethingTo`; `PZMath.degToRad`/`radToDeg`/`getClosestAngle`.

The 24 scenarios (all in `tools/oracle.sh`): attack range (`zai:<seed>:60..200:0.6:0:force`, 5 seeds): idle until tick 5, then `AttackState` (`ctx=attack`, `AttackType` bite from the next tick) for 5 ticks, back to `ZombieIdleState` on tick 10, again attack on tick 15, and so on with a period of 10 (`canSeeTarget` stays false headless, so the zombie's own `bAttack` transition keeps dropping out); a target at 0.6 to the south, north or west faces it first (`ctx=face-target`, `ZombieFaceTargetState`, `faceThisObject` every tick, the twist going to its limit of 15 degrees and `isTurningAround` raising the `Turn180Started` global anim event); a target at 0.6 to the south-east and targets at 2 to 3 squares east, south, west, north (9 ticks: idle, then `LungeState` from tick 5); forced targets that cannot be lunged at (`-1,-1` and `-2,+2`: behind a wall to the west, `lineClearCollide` blocked; `5,5`: farther than 3.5; 60 ticks): `bLunge` false and `ZombieIdleState` only, 3 draws on tick 5 (the idle state's own); no target for 1,389 to 2,269 ticks (seeds 12345, 7, 2, 12, 3, 1, 9): the wander event at a seed-dependent tick (none within 2,000 ticks for seed 7) sets `goal=Location`, then `pathToAux`: a clear line sets `bMoving=true`, `bPathfind=false` and the state becomes `WalkTowardState` on the next animation frame, a tick that is a multiple of 5 (seeds 3, 1, 9: ticks 1385, 2265, 1770; these three E62 scenarios end 4 ticks later, before the first deferred movement; E65a runs the same walks on for 3,000 ticks), a blocked one sets `bPathfind=true` and the state becomes `PathFindState` (seeds 12345, 2, 12: ticks 1635, 1310, 1980), where it stays: the finder's progress never leaves `notyetfound`, 2 draws per tick (`Rand.Next(1000)` of the animation frame increase and `damageSheetRope` `Rand.Next(30)`). No scenario includes the zombie's deferred movement (see UNKNOWN), so `x`/`y` are compared exactly as the original prints them.

Measured on the way (each a line of the oracle): the first animation update frame is tick 5 and the `AnimationPlayer` is made there (angle 0, `maxTwist` 15 degrees); `getLookAngle` is the animation player's `angle + twist` once it exists and the forward direction's angle before; `isBoneTransformsNeedFirstFrame` stays true headless so `animAngle` equals `angle`; reading `numtwistbones`, `grappleanim`, `anygrappleanim`, `canragdoll`, `isupright`, `isonback` or `useragdollvehiclecollision` creates the animation player (the probe prints `?` before tick 5 and reads them afterwards: 5 twist bones, `canRagdoll` true for a zombie in a state, the others false); `bMovingNetwork` is a constant true; `GameServer.server` false changes nothing in the construction's 22 draws.

NOT PORTED / UNKNOWN (each is an `Err` where the original would run it on state this port can reach, never a guessed value): (E65a ported the deferred root movement, the collision passes and `separate()` for the player stand-in: see the next paragraph), `ensureOnTile`, `RespondToSound` (assumed: no world sound exists), `ZombieGroupManager.update` for a lone zombie, `tryThump` (the feeler is the zombie's own square), vehicles, doors and windows on the line, stairs and sloped squares (`Err`), `OnZombieUpdate` and `OnAIStateChange` Lua handlers, `NetworkZombieAI.extraUpdate`/`mindSync`, `ParameterZombieState` audio, `canSeeTarget` (the squares' `couldSee` is never computed headless: always false), `IsoZombie.spotted` (it never acquires a target headless: no scenario sets one without `force`), a found path (`PathFindState` never completes headless; the port returns `Err` for `Found`/`Failed`/`Succeeded`), `WalkTowardState` with a target or arrival within 0.25 of the path target (`Err`), `AttackState` with a defence test, a sprinter's slow factor or `AttackCollisionCheck` against the player (the bite: `Err`), the crawler variants, `lastTargetSeenX` paths.

E65a, deferred (animation root) movement, collision and `separate` (2026-11-18; `zombieai` 36,893 lines identical, `zombie` 2,997, `body` 738, `animsets` 91,705, `climate` 306,807 unchanged). The chain is ported literally: every 5th tick the animation frame (dt 5/60) runs `AnimationTrack.updateDeferredValues` (the deferred bone `Translation_Data`, axis Y: the movement is `(x, -z)` of the key frames, counter-rotated by the track's rotation when `useDeferredRotation`), `AnimationPlayer.calculateDeferredMovementAccumInternal` blends the live track entries (sorted by layer, priority and weight, from the top down until the remaining weight is at most 0.001; `applyRotationToDeferredMovement` with `renderedAngle = angle + PI / 2`; times the `MotionScale` of `media/AnimSets/Defaults.xml`, read from the file), the next animation-updating `IsoZombie.update` takes it in `IsoGameCharacter.doDeferredMovement` (`getDeferredMovement(result, true)`, the `WalkTowardState` clamp to the path target) into `IsoZombie.moveUnmodded`, and `IsoMovingObject.postupdate` turns `nextX/nextY` into the position (the two `DoCollide` passes with the favoured axis alternating from tick to tick, the second pass after a collision with `len`/`lnx`/`lny`, `getGlobalMovementMod`, `setX/setY`, `doStairs`, `handleSlopedSurface`, `findCurrentGridSquare`, `snapZ`) before `postUpdateAnimating`. The key frames come from the game's own clips (E59: `native::assimp::import_file` plus `ProcessedAiScene::process` with `LoadMode::AnimationOnly`, skinned to the Human animation skeleton; the `AnimationClips` table by name from `anims_X/{Bob,Kate,Zombie}`), `fdlibm` `cos/sin` for `Vector2.rotate`, the `HelperFunctions` quaternion angles and slerp. Measured on the way: (1) collision reads each square's STORED `collideMatrix`: `IsoChunk.loadInWorldStreamerThread` computes it with a `ChunkGetter` that knows only the chunk's own squares, so every bit that points over a chunk border stays blocked (the initial 134217727), and the main-thread border recalculation (`IsoChunk` lines 2735-2835) never runs in the probe: a zombie lunging south at the player stops at `y = 11671.573` in front of the border `y = 11672` between chunks 1458 and 1459; `zsq` prints the collide, path and vision matrices of every square of the four chunks (340 lines) and Rust and Java agree byte for byte; (2) `separate()` (the player stand-in on the zombie's square or on a square of its `nav`, which `doGridNav` linked within the chunk, not blocked: `len < width + width` pushes `nextX/nextY` away by `(twidth - len) / 8`; `isPushableForSeparate` is false in `AttackState` and `ZombieFaceTargetState`) matches at contact in the same chunk (`zai:10:300:0.3:0.3:force`) and across the chunk border (`zai:6:300:-0.7:0:force`); (3) `getGlobalMovementMod` sets `RenderEffectType.Vegetation_Rustle` on the trees and bushes of the zombie's square and of the feeler square (`isBush`: sprite tileset `f_bushes_1` or the property `Bush`), and with no effect set yet `ObjectRenderEffects.getNew` draws `Rand.Next(45.0F, 60.0F)` once per object (single-player: `GameServer.server` is false); the headless world never updates the object, so the effect stays and a bush draws once per run (`zai:1:200:0:2.0:force`: one extra draw on tick 180); (4) `WalkTowardState.isMoving` is true, so a walking zombie whose target twist passes one degree starts the turning flags (`isBehaviourMoving`; found by `zai:9:3000:3.0:0:none`, tick 1815); (5) the probe leaked state between the scenarios of one JVM (the zombie stayed in the `getMovingObjects()` list of every square it left, a ghost that pushed the next zombie by `separate`; a rustled bush kept its effect): the probe now removes both after every scenario (an artifact of the oracle, not of the game). Proof (registered): `zsq`, twelve forced scenarios (`zai:12345:200:2.0:0:force`, `zai:1|2|3:200`, `zai:4|5:300`, `zai:12345:400:5.0:5.0`, `zai:6:300:-0.7:0`, `zai:7:300:0.45:0.2`, `zai:10:400:1.0:1.0`, `zai:13:400:4.0:-3.0`, `zai:10:300:0.3:0.3`: lunge, attack, the push after it, contact, the stall at the chunk border) and four 3,000-tick wander walks with turns (`zai:3|1|9|2:3000:3.0:0:none`); 12 more scenarios (seeds 6 to 13, other directions, `zai:12:3000`, `zai:12345:3000`, `zai:11:300:-1.0:-1.0:force`) were compared in a scratch run and match. NOT PORTED (each an `Err` where the original would run it): a collision whose squares carry a hoppable, collide, window or door flag (`checkBreakHoppable`, `checkHitHoppable`, `checkBreakBendableFence`, `climbOverFence`, `Collided`'s Lua event, `testCollideSpecialObjects`), a crawling zombie, stairs and sloped surfaces, `virtualizeZombie` (a zombie leaving the loaded area on a path), a zombie without a square, `doTreeNoises` (only collects the square for the tree sound manager, no state the oracle reads), the `OnCharacterCollide` Lua event (no handler exists in `media/lua`), `separate` against anything but the player stand-in (no second moving object exists), a vehicle (`PolygonalMap2.resolveCollision` returns the position it is given without one).

E65b, the melee hit, StaggerBackState, ZombieFallDownState, ZombieOnGroundState, ZombieGetUpState (2026-11-20; `sh tools/oracle.sh zombiehit` -> `OK   zombiehit: 16678 lines identical`, 44 scenarios, about 2 minutes; `tests/oracle_hashes.rs::zombiehit_matches_original`; `zombieai`, `zombie`, `actioncontext`, `body`, `animsets`, `climate` unchanged, see PORT.md). `ZombieAiProbe` takes `hit<N>[,<M>...][@<weapon>[@<damage split>[@c]]]` as the mode (default weapon `Base.BaseballBat`, split 1.0, `c` = the wielder's critical hit flag): like `force`, and at those ticks the player stand-in hits the zombie through the REAL `IsoZombie.Hit(weapon, wielder, damageSplit, false, 1.0f, false)` (the item is made by `InventoryItemFactory.CreateItem` first, as the game does when a weapon is equipped: its draws come before the hit's). Ported literally (`zombie/characters/iso_zombie_hit.rs`, `iso_zombie_fall.rs`, `iso_zombie_states.rs`): `IsoZombie.Hit` (the `hitTime` damage scaling of repeated hits, `staggerTimeMod`, the AI state test that sends a zombie in `AttackState`/`LungeState` within 0.9 to `StaggerBackState`), `IsoGameCharacter.Hit`/`calculateHitDirection`/`processHitDamage` (every modifier, in order, with `int`/`float` widths), `hitConsequences` (`applyGlobalDamageReductionMultipliers` 0.15, `applyDamage`, `splatBlood(2, 0.2F)` for weapons with `splatBloodOnNoDeath`, the `wasHit` event, `CombatManager.processHit`/`calculateShotDirection`/`applyBlood` and the wall/floor splat draws, `setTarget`, `setKnockedDown(critical || onFloor)`, `shouldBecomeCrawler`'s `NextBool(30)`), `StaggerBackState`, `ZombieFallDownState`, `ZombieOnGroundState` (the reanimate timer `Rand.Next(60) + 30`, `isBeingSteppedOn`), `ZombieGetUpState`, the anim events `FallOnFront`, `SetOnFloor`, `SetKnockedDown`, `IsAlmostUp`, `PlaySound`. Measured on the way (each a line of the oracle): (1) a hit's draws come in this order: the item creation (`Next(MAX_INT)` for the id, `Next(n)` of `synchWithVisual` for a weapon with a model list, `Next(2146250223)`), body part `Next(2)`, `NextBool(7)`, `NextBool(3)` on a critical hit, the splat draws of an axe or knife, `usePhysicHitReaction`'s `nextFloat`, `calculateShotDirection` `Next(9)`/`Next(4)`, `applyBlood` `Next(6)`, `shouldBecomeCrawler`, then the tick's own draws; (2) `IsoZombie.setEatBodyTarget(null, false)` evaluates `Rand.Next(0.64F, 0.96F)` for its `eatSpeed` argument whatever the target is: one `nextFloat` when `ZombieFallDownState` and `ZombieOnGroundState` are entered; (3) the `PlaySound` anim event (`BodyHitGround` at 50% of every fall-down clip) draws `Rand.Next(clips.size())` in `GameSound.getRandomClip` (`BodyHitGround` has one clip, so `nextInt(1)`; `GameSounds.getSound` makes a one-clip sound for a name no script has; `addSound` draws nothing); it reads the port's own `GameSounds` (E64's scripts); (4) the player stand-in answers the hit chain with `getShovingMod` 0.75, no traits, endurance 1, `knockbackAttackMod` 1, weapon level 0, not shoving, not aiming at the floor, forward (-1, 0), empty hands (a two-handed weapon gets the 0.5 one-handed penalty); (5) a plain hit of a zombie in `AttackState`/`LungeState` within reach: `StaggerBackState` for 20..30 ticks of `stateEventDelayTimer`, then `ZombieIdleState`, `LungeState`, `AttackState` again; a critical hit: `staggerback-knockeddown` (close range) or `lunge` -> `falldown` (`ZombieFallDownState`, tick 20) -> `onground` (`ZombieOnGroundState`, tick 105: the clip's `SetOnFloor`) -> `getup-fromOnBack`/`FromOnFront` once `reanimateTimer` (30..89 ticks, counted down by the 30 fps multiplier) is spent; (6) in the headless world the animation player has a valid pose: the original restarts the reanimate timer (a `Next(60)` draw at tick 189 of the player-0.5-away scenario) because `isBeingSteppedOn` finds the player stand-in standing on the lying zombie (`PZ_RANDTRACE`: the draw is `ZombieOnGroundState.execute` -> `startReanimateTimer`). The scenarios: 20 plain hits (bat, axe, knife, pipe, bare hands, seeds 11 and 12, the player 2.0 and 0.5 away, 150 ticks), 5 critical hits (200 ticks), 6 critical hits that run on to the get-up (400 ticks, player 3.0 or (4, -3) away), 8 hits from other directions (north, west, south, south-east, north-west, close range, a hit with 25 ticks of delay) and 5 with repeated hits (2 to 5 hits, a damage split of 0.5). UNKNOWN / not ported (each an `Err`, never a guessed value): the kill (`health <= 0`: `IsoGameCharacter.Kill`, `die()`, `becomeCorpse`, `IsoDeadBody`, the zombie's drops, the corpse's inventory and the `OnZombieDead` Lua event; measured by exploration, not an oracle scenario: `zai:61:120:2.0:0:hit20@Base.BaseballBat@100.0` gives damage 22.5, health 0, dead, 43 draws on the hit tick (63 for `zai:62:120:2.0:0:hit20@Base.Axe@100.0@c`), the action context `falldown` from the hit tick and `onground` from tick 105 with no reanimate timer draw), therefore `ZombieEatBodyState` (it needs a corpse) and `setEatBodyTarget` with a target; `isBeingSteppedOn` while the player stand-in is within 1.92 of a lying zombie (it tests the world positions of the spine, calf and head bones: `Model.boneToWorldCoords` needs the animation player's bone pose, `AnimationPlayer.Update` beyond the track times is not ported; the 0.5-away scenarios therefore stop before the reanimate timer runs out, `zai:47:180`); the conversion to a crawler (`shouldBecomeCrawler` is drawn; when it is true `ZombieOnGroundState` returns `Err`: stage 3), a Sharpness other than 1.0, ranged and explosive weapons (the ballistics path), a shove, traits and perks of the wielder, the `OnHitZombie` Lua handler's visual damage, the `IsoWallBloodSplat` objects (their draws are reproduced, the objects are not kept), `ThumpState` (`IsoThumpable`), `ClimbOverFenceState`, `BumpedState` and the other states: no scenario so far reaches them.

## ActionContext and animator runtime by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh actioncontext` -> `OK   actioncontext: 143826 lines identical` (about 3 minutes); `zombie` (2997 lines), `animsets` (91705) and `climate` (306807) stay identical. `ActionContextProbe` runs the original classes inside the booted dedicated server (`ServerHarness`) and `pz-dump actioncontext` prints the port's state in the same text; the scenarios are in `tools/oracle.sh` and `tests/oracle_hashes.rs::actioncontext_matches_original`. Ported (each fn opens with the Java method it follows): `zombie.characters.action.ActionContext` (state, child states, previous state and history, `peekNextState`, `canTransitionToState`, events with their per-state scope, the states-changed callbacks, `setGroup`), `ActionStateContainer`/`ActionContextEvents`, the condition runtime (`CharacterVariableCondition`, `EventOccurred`/`EventNotOccurred`, `LuaCall` as UNKNOWN), the `AnimationVariableSource` slots (`Bool`, `String`, `Float`) and handle pool, `AdvancedAnimator` (`init`, `setState` with sub layers, `update`, `reset`, `GenerateTransitionData`/`ProcessTransitions` through `TransitionNodeProxy`, the while-alive flag counters, `cleanUpEmptyLayers`), `AnimLayer` (`transitionTo`, `updateLiveAnimNodes` with `AnimState.getAnimNodes` and `AnimNode.checkConditions`, `Anim2DBlendPicker`, `startLiveNodeTracks` with its draws, `updateInternal`, `getMaximumTwist`, the local events), `LiveAnimNode` (weights, blend in/out, transition in/out, main tracks, random anims, `getMainInitialRewindTime`), `SubLayerSlot`, `AnimationTrack` (time, looping, `UpdateKeyframes`, track events), `AnimationClip` durations (the `.X` reader: 1844 clips, identical to the Java table, scenario `clips`) and `zombie.util.FrameDelay`.

The zombie (`IsoZombie::{preupdate, postupdate}`): `updateAnimationTimeDelta` (a `FrameDelay(4)`: the animation updates every 5th tick with dt 5/60 = 0.083333336, other ticks 1/60, `animationTimeScale` 5 or 1), then `ActionContext.update` with its callbacks (`AdvancedAnimator.setState`, the AI state machine `changeState`), `AdvancedAnimator.update`, the `ActiveAnim*` events cleared, `AnimationPlayer.Update` (the track times and their listeners). **The E57 MEASURED-ONLY draw is gone**: the third draw of the first tick is `AnimLayer.startLiveNodeTracks` `randomAlpha = Rand.Next(0, 1)` of the live node `defaultIdle`, derived from `media/AnimSets/zombie`; tick 1 draws 3 times (`damageSheetRope`, the idle wander interval, `randomAlpha`), one per tick after, as before (`zombie` is byte-identical, same hash). Measured on the real zombie (`zanim:12345:1200`, `zanim:7:600`): tick 1 live node `defaultIdle` weight 0.0045351484 (raw 0.047619052, blending in), track `Zombie_Idle` time 0.004027848 speed 0.24167086 (seed 12345); the weight is 0.99546486 at tick 12 (ticks 5 and 10 are the 5/60 updates).

The animator is proven by scripted-character fuzz (`anim:<set>:<seed>:<ticks>[:states]`): the real `AdvancedAnimator` of an animation set on a `Proxy` character (variables = a real `AnimationVariableSource`, events logged, the player a zombie's `AnimationPlayer` after `reset()`), driven by a seeded `java.util.Random` that sets the variables the set's conditions read (every condition of every node and transition: 47 variables for `zombie`, 129 for `player`), clears some, picks root and sub states (also unknown names, which `AnimationSet.GetState` turns into a new empty `AnimState`), and calls `AdvancedAnimator.reset` now and then, with 8 dt values. Per tick it prints the draw count, root state, every layer's live nodes (weights, blend flags, transition-in) and every track (time, speed, blend and field weight, flags) and every event with its layer and state. 9,900 scripted ticks over the sets `zombie`, `zombie-crawler`, `player`, `player-vehicle` in 7 scenarios (and 1,800 real-zombie ticks in 2): in the whole case 68 distinct event names, 436 distinct nodes, 10,895 sub layer lines, 360 ticks with a transition-in track (`player` transitions with `m_AnimName`; the `zombie` set has transitions without clips), 4,549 blend-field node lines (2D blend pickers), 910 node lines with `FlagWhileAlive` flags, `maxTwist` writes, 77 unknown-state selections, 95 resets, ticks with up to 5 draws (random anims, `randomAdvanceFraction`, ragdoll start window, `randomAlpha`). A scripted run on the `mannequin` set (clips that do not exist: `Anim Clip not found`) was identical too; not in the case.

Debug options the port assumes (printed by the probe as `OPTS`, and compared): `Core.debug` false, `allowEarlyTransitionOut` true, `disableAnimationBlends` false, `physicsHitReaction` true, `logStateChanges` false, `allowAnimNodeOverride` false.

Measured facts about the server zombie: every `IsoZombie.setVariable` overload is a no-op on a dedicated server, so its game variable store is empty (`getGameVariables()` is 0 on every tick) and the only variables the conditions can read are the action states' own. The zombie's ActionContext therefore stays in `idle` (AI `ZombieIdleState`) in every tick of a real run; a target (`setTarget(player)` after `ServerLOS.init()` with `timeSinceSeenFlesh = 0`, the player on a square; 400 ticks, exploration) changes nothing: ctx `idle`, no child state, target kept, position unchanged. A zombie whose target is not refreshed loses it in the first `update` (`timeSinceSeenFlesh > memory`). Only event conditions can fire its ActionContext on this server; the exits from idle lead to unported AI states. A dropped track listener on a released layer: a transition track keeps the layer that started it as a listener (`startTransitionAnimation` on the old node's layer) and outlives it; the original's `AnimLayer.onReleased` clears `parentAnimator`, so its events do nothing (the port ignores them; found by the fuzz).

NOT PORTED / UNKNOWN (each returns `Err` where the original would run it on state this port can reach, never a guessed value): bone poses and bone masks (`AnimationPlayer.Update` beyond the track times), (the deferred (root) movement of a track is ported since E65a; `AnimationPlayer.DoAngles`/`updateForwardDirection`/turning flags since E62), ragdoll nodes and the ragdoll controller (`startRagdollTrack`; ragdoll states are left out of the fuzz), node blend curves (`track.blendCurve`; only `onground-ragdoll-breathing` has one), grappling (`updateInternalGrapple`, the grappler/grappled nodes), the animation recorder and the debug monitor, `IsoGameCharacter.OnAnimEvent` handlers other than the no-op ones (`PlaySound`, `Footstep`, `SetVariable` on a zombie, ... return `Err` in the zombie; the scripted character logs them), `NetworkZombieAI.extraUpdate` on state changes, `ActionContext` `LuaCall` conditions, the AI states other than `ZombieIdleState` (E62 ported `LungeState`, `WalkTowardState`, `PathFindState`, `AttackState` and `ZombieFaceTargetState` for the single-player zombie, see "Zombie AI states by direct port"; `StaggerBackState`, `ZombieFallDownState`, `ZombieOnGroundState`, ... (ported for the single-player zombie by E65b): the dedicated-server zombie port returns `Err` when the action context leaves idle), crawling zombies (the `zombie-crawler` set is proven by the fuzz only), the pre-sort order of the `Anim2DBlendPicker` hull when two clips are equal and the precedence of duplicate clip names in the `.X` table (no duplicates exist in the shipped files), the original's `Pool` reuse of released layers/nodes/tracks (a released layer that is allocated again would receive the events of an old track; the port gives every object a new id), and the track events: the original calls a listener from inside the track's tick, the port collects the events of one track's tick and dispatches them after it (a listener sees the track's final time; none of the ported ones reads it, the fuzz found no difference). Stage "hit/knockdown on the real zombie" is done for the single-player zombie by E65b (`IsoZombie.Hit`, `StaggerBackState`, `ZombieFallDownState`, `ZombieOnGroundState`, `ZombieGetUpState`; oracle `zombiehit`, see "Zombie AI states by direct port"); on the dedicated-server zombie those transitions stay covered by the `ctx:zombie` ActionContext scenarios (28,042 lines, E60a) and the animator fuzz on the `zombie` states they lead to.

## Zombies by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh zombie` -> `OK   zombie: 2997 lines identical` (about 2 minutes; the 9 long scenarios run 6,000 to 15,000 update ticks each). The original `zombie.characters.IsoZombie` runs headless inside the booted dedicated server (`ZombieProbe` in `ServerHarness`) against `pz-dump zombie`. The probe sets all twelve `ZombieLore` options itself to a fixed preset (speed 2, strength 2, cognition 3, memory 2, sight 2, hearing 2, `ActiveOnly` 1, ... the booted server's own preset is speed 4, sight 5, hearing 5, so it must not be relied on), seeds the global `Rand` with `java.util.Random(seed)` (draw counter `RandAbstract.id` at 0) and `OutfitRNG` likewise.

Stage (a), construction (`IsoZombie(IsoCell)`, 11 scenarios with 350 zombies, plus the 9 one-zombie tick scenarios: 359 zombies in all; 21 to 25 global draws per zombie, 22 for the first zombie of seed 12345 under the preset), in the original's order: `IsoGameCharacter` patience (`Rand.Next(20, 150)`), health `1.8F + Rand.Next(0, 0.3F)`, `IsoDirections.getRandom`, `HumanVisual.randomBlood` (18 `OutfitRNG.Next(0, 1)`, not the global Rand), `SurvivorFactory.CreateSurvivor` (type `Next(3)`, gender `Next(2)`, `SurvivorDesc.doStats` 6 draws, `randomName` 2), palette, `InitSpritePartsZombie` (`zombieId = Rand.Next(10000)`), three tints, `DoZombieStats` (cognition/strength/memory/sight/hearing branches of the lore options, `doZombieSpeed` with `determineZombieSpeed`, sprinter/fast shambler/fake shambler/shambler, walk type, `speedMod`), `initCanCrawlUnderVehicle`. The branches exercised (as printed per zombie): speedType 1, 2 and 3 (75, 228, 56 zombies), strength 1/3/5 and the random 1..4, memory 25/500/800/1250 through the fixed and the two random settings, sight and hearing 1..3 (fixed and random settings), cognition -1/0/1, crawl-under chances 0/5/10/25/50/75/100 %. The name lists (`SurvivorFactory.FemaleForenames` 1320, `MaleForenames` 993, `Surnames` 3799) are the effect of `media/lua/shared/NPCs/MainCreationMethods.lua` (`OnGameBoot`) with the game's translations; the Rust side reads that file's `addSurname`/`addFemaleForename`/`addMaleForename` lines (STAND-IN for running the Lua on the ported VM, named as such in `SurvivorFactory::from_main_creation_methods`) and the first, middle and last name of each list are in the oracle.

Stage (b), the idle zombie (`IsoZombie.update` with no target; 9 scenarios, 6,000..15,000 ticks): one zombie alone on the square (8280, 11670, 0) of the loaded chunks 1034..1035 x 1458..1459 (no player, no sound, no other zombie, a dedicated server's `GameTime.getMultiplier()` 0.8, not raining), `preupdate(); update(); postupdate();` per tick. Measured and ported: the tick order (on the first tick `update` runs with no AI state and only draws `damageSheetRope`'s `Rand.Next(30)`; the action system enters ZombieIdleState in `postupdate`, which draws `pickRandomWanderInterval` = `Rand.Next(400, 1000)` x 1.5 when not raining), one global draw per tick afterwards, the state timer counting down by `getThirtyFPSMultiplier()` (0.5) while `timeSinceSeenFlesh` grows by `getMultiplier()` (0.8), `timeSinceRespondToSound` and `checkForCorpseTimer` by 0.5, `allowRepathDelay` clamped down by 0.8, and the wander event of `ZombieIdleState.execute` when the timer reaches 0 (new interval, `Rand.Next(8) - 4` twice, `IsoGridSquare.isFree(true)` of the square, then `PathFindBehavior2.pathToLocation` and `allowRepathDelay = 200`): 38 events in the scenarios, 32 with a free square (goal `Location`, target x/y + 0.5) and 6 on a square that is not free (3 draws, no path). The position never changes (movement is animation driven and no animation runs headless) and the state stays idle.

Measured and NOT ported (UNKNOWN beyond the oracle): (resolved by E60: the `AnimLayer.startLiveNodeTracks` draw of one float (`randomAlpha = Rand.Next(0, 1)`) on the first state entry is now derived from the AnimSets by the ported animator, the MEASURED-ONLY constant is removed; see "ActionContext and animator runtime by direct port".) `IsoGameCharacter.pathToAux` (`PolygonalMap2.lineClearCollide`, then `setMoving`/`bPathfind`): after a wander path the original reads `moving=true`, `bPathfind=false` and still does not leave idle (on a dedicated server every `setVariable` is a no-op; E62 ported the single-player pathToAux, where the same wander goes on to `WalkTowardState`/`PathFindState`). `RespondToSound` is a no-op under `GameServer.server`; `ZombieGroupManager.update` ran every tick (timeSinceSeenFlesh > 2000) and left no trace in what is printed for a lone zombie; `separate`, `tryThump(null)`, `ensureOnTile`, the corpse scan (the Rust tick returns `Err` when `checkForCorpseTimer` reaches 0, after 20,000 ticks), sheet ropes (`Err`), farming plants and campfires of the square, `OnZombieUpdate` Lua handlers (no draws seen), and `GameTime.isZombieInactivityPhase` with `ActiveOnly` other than 1 (`Err`; it needs the season's dawn and dusk).

Stage (c), target acquisition and attack: BLOCKED on the dedicated server, with evidence (exploration only, not an oracle case; resolved by E62 for the single-player zombie, see "Zombie AI states by direct port"). A `new IsoPlayer(cell, null, x, y, z)` can be made in the booted server (ID 2, `numPlayers` 1), but `IsoZombie.spotted(player, true)` four squares away sets no target; with `ServerLOS.init()` (`ServerLOS.instance` is null on this server, so `IsoZombie.update` throws a NullPointerException as soon as a target is set) and `setTarget(player)` + `timeSinceSeenFlesh = 0`, 400 ticks later the zombie is still in ZombieIdleState (action state "idle", not moving, position unchanged, target kept). The transitions to WalkTowardState/LungeState/AttackState/PathFindState come from the `zombie` ActionGroup's transitions evaluated by the ActionContext (E60: now ported); they do not fire here because the server zombie's game variables are empty (E60 measured `getGameVariables()` = 0, ctx stays `idle` for 400 ticks with the target kept: the `ServerLOS.init()` NullPointerException and `timeSinceSeenFlesh` need setting as above); sight (`spotted`) reads the square's `lighting[playerIndex]` (a client structure) and the `LosUtil` results of the player's chunk map. Everything about those states, `ZombiePopulationManager` (native), `VirtualZombieManager.createRealZombie`/`addZombieToWorld`, `ZombiesZoneDefinition` (outfit and spawn rules: read in full, not ported), `ZombieGroupManager`, `MovingObjectUpdateScheduler` and `dressInRandomOutfit` is UNKNOWN / not ported.

## Erosion by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh erosion` -> `OK   erosion: 51097 lines identical` (about 40 s). The original `zombie.erosion.*` runs inside the booted dedicated server (`ErosionProbe` in `ServerHarness`, committed d739da1) against the port (`pz-dump erosion`). Per scenario the probe sets the sandbox options (`ErosionSpeed`, `ErosionDays`, `TimeSinceApo`, `Temperature`) and the date, deletes the save's and the cache's `erosion.ini` (unless `keep=1`), makes a new `ErosionMain` (config, the four noises, the season, the ice queen, the regions with their eight categories), loads chunks of the real map with `CellLoader.LoadCellBinaryChunk` (global `Rand` on `java.util.Random(12345)`) and `IsoChunk.loadInWorldStreamerThread`, puts them into `ServerMap.cellMap` as loaded cells, and runs the server's order: every square with objects through `ErosionMain.LoadGridsquare` and every chunk through `ChunkLoaded` (the order of `ServerCell.RecalcAll2`/`IsoChunk.doLoadGridsquare`), then per simulated day the date advances and `ErosionMain.EveryTenMinutes` (`mainTimer`) runs 144 times. Compared per scenario: the set-up line (options, `ticks`/`eTicks`/`epoch`/`tickUnit`, date, season, season day/days/progression, snow, number of sprites the `ErosionMain` constructor created), the draw counter of the global `Rand` before the load, after it and at the end (always 0: erosion takes no `Rand` on a server), at each dump day the same state plus, per chunk, `ErosionData.Chunk.save` (hex) with moisture and minerals, and per square with objects, sorted by x, y, z: `ErosionData.Square.save` (hex: the noise bytes, soil, magic number and every region's category data), the kudzu noise (float bits), `haveRoof`, the property flags in `getFlagsList` order, `hasTypes`, and every object as class, name, sprite and attached sprites (a tree with its `size`); at the end the `erosion.ini` the original wrote (every line).

Scenarios (all in `tools/oracle.sh` and `tests/oracle_hashes.rs`, 19, 43 dumps, 49,496 squares): the load alone of a 6 by 6 chunk stretch of Rosewood; the same from October 1993 for 200 days (autumn, winter, spring: ErosionSpeed 1, dumps every 40 days); the flowerbeds' chunks (1017..1020 by 1418..1421) and a town (1327..1332 by 1285..1290) from April 1993 for 100 days; default options (speed 3) over 400 days of a 3 by 3 stretch; `speed` 2, 4, 5; `edays` 30 and -1; `apo` 7 and 13 (with speed 5); `temp` 1, 2, 4, 5; and a chain of three scenarios that read back the `erosion.ini` the previous one wrote (`keep=1`: the file wins over the options). All eight categories grow objects in them: trees (`NatureTrees`), bushes, plants and flowers (`NatureBush`, `NaturePlants`), grass (`NatureGeneric`), `Flowerbed`, `d_streetcracks` (676 sprites), `d_wallcracks` (319), `f_wallvines` (451).

Ported (`crates/zomboid-engine/src/zombie/erosion/`): `ErosionMain` (constructor/`start`/`initConfig` for a server with the speed's scaling of the tick unit, `TimeSinceApo`, `ErosionDays`, the temperature modifier on the season; the four `Noise2D` layer sets; `loadGridsquare`, `initGridSquare`, `getChunk`, `initChunk` with `soilTable`, `loadChunk`, `mainTimer` with its date change and the per-chunk re-run, the `erosion.ini` text it writes), `ErosionConfig` (E53's `readFile`/`writeFile`, taken as it is on main; the speed scaling and `TimeSinceApo` are applied in `ErosionMain.initConfig`), `ErosionData` (`Chunk` and `Square`: `set`, `save`, `load`, the category data of every category, `rand` on a server), `ErosionRegions` (the four regions, their categories, `getCategory`), `ErosionWorld` (`init`, `validateSpawn`, `update`), `ErosionCategory` and the eight categories (`NatureTrees`, `NatureBush`, `NaturePlants`, `NatureGeneric`, `StreetCracks`, `WallCracks`, `WallVines`, `Flowerbed`), `ErosionObj`, `ErosionObjSprites`, `ErosionObjOverlay`, `ErosionObjOverlaySprites`, `ErosionIceQueen` (the winter sprites it makes and `setSnow`, which only `ClimateManager` calls: no scenario turns snow on, `snow=0` throughout); and what the erosion run needs of the iso layer: `IsoChunk.loadInWorldStreamerThread` (squares around objects, `assignRoom`, `ensureNotNull`, `haveRoof` under rain blocking tiles, the dirty flag and the neighbour `RecalcAllWithNeighbours`), `ServerMap` (cells, `getGridSquare` through loaded cells, `AddTileObject`, `RemoveTileObject`, `RemoveTileObjectErosionNoRecalc`, `IsoCell.checkHaveRoof`), `IsoGridSquare` (`erosion` data, the property-dirty rules of `RecalcProperties`).

Measured on the way (each a line of the oracle):
- Erosion on a server draws no global `Rand`: `ErosionData.Square.rand` reads a static 90 by 90 table of percentages (`rands[x % 90 + y % 90 * 90]`) and `min((int) (r * max), max - 1)`. The Rust side reads that table from the compiled `zombie/erosion/ErosionData$Square.class` of the player's own `projectzomboid.jar` when it runs (the `zip` crate was added to `zomboid-engine` for it, like `zomboid-content` uses it); no game data is in the source.
- `IsoTree.setSprite` calls `initTree` again, so a tree's `size`, log yield and damage come from the `tree` property of the sprite of its current erosion stage (a map tree `vegetation_trees_01_8` of size 2 becomes size 3 or 4 when `NatureTrees` gives it `e_dogwood_1_2` or `_3`), and `initTree` also sets the sprite's tile type to `tree` on the shared sprite.
- `ErosionGlobals.Boot` (from `IsoWorld.init`) has already made an `ErosionMain` before any probe runs, with the ice queen's 90 winter sprites; the scenarios' own `ErosionMain` creates none (`newSprites=0` in all 62 lines). `pz-dump erosion` makes that boot `ErosionMain` first for the same reason.
- `media/data/erosion.ini` does not exist in the install, so the config is the defaults changed by the sandbox options until a save has an `erosion.ini`; `mainTimer` writes it at every tick (the probe prints the last text). `ErosionMain.epoch` is not read from the file.
- `media/lua/shared/Util/CustomTileProps.lua` (`OnServerStarted`) changes sprite properties before a probe runs (the bathtubs' `waterPiped`, bed types, materials); the square flags of the oracle show it. The ported Lua VM does not expose `IsoSpriteManager` yet, so `pz-dump erosion` reads the three tables of that file at run time and applies them in the file's order (`SetPair`, `RemovePair`, `SetFlag`); a statement of another shape is an error.

UNKNOWN / not ported: the client side of `ErosionMain` (`GameClient` branches, `sendState`, `receiveState`, `DebugUpdateMapNow`, `updateMapNow`, the config over the network), `ErosionData.Square.rand` outside a server (`RandLocation`), the Lua event `OnInitSeasons` (only `LastStand/WinterIsComing.lua` listens), `snowCheck` (empty in the original), the roof-snow setters of the ice queen (nothing calls them), `consolePrint`; of the chunk load, the border squares of a server cell (`EnsureSurroundNotNull` and `RecalcAllWithNeighbours` across cells), the collide, path and vision matrices of the neighbour recalculation, the other steps of `IsoChunk.doLoadGridsquare` (grass regrowth, the Lua `LoadGridsquare` handlers, zombies, `OnObjectAboutToBeRemoved` handlers, barricades), and `IsoChunk.save`/`load` integration of the erosion data beyond the bytes (`ErosionData.Chunk.save/load` and `Square.save/load` are ported and compared as hex). The order in which `ErosionIceQueen` visits its sprite `HashMap` is not defined by the source: the port visits in creation order, which only decides the order in which missing winter sprites would be created (the boot made them all, so no scenario creates any). Whether other Lua of the install changes sprites at server start is UNKNOWN (the oracle shows none beyond `CustomTileProps.lua` in the squares it covers: Rosewood and a town, with trees, bushes, plants, flowers, flowerbeds, street cracks, wall cracks and wall vines).

## Loot generation (ItemPicker) by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh loot` -> `OK   loot: 339123 lines identical`. The original `ItemPickerJava` (`LootProbe`, a `game_case`: `LuaGameProbe.init` with the shared and server Lua, the three distribution merge events, `Parse`, `InitSandboxLootSettings`, `StashSystem.init`) against the port (`pz-dump loot`). Stage a (`Parse` and the tables) compares the 61672 lines of the parsed tables as before. Stage b compares 5155 scenarios of `tests/loot_scenarios.txt` (61025 containers filled one after another from one seeded `java.util.Random`, as `climate` swaps its generator; 206116 `ITEM` lines; 142 fills end in a Java exception that the port reproduces): per container the `RandAbstract.id` draws it took, the item count and the items' full types in order (`EXC <class>` when the original throws, e.g. the `NullPointerException` of `Clothing.getVisual()`), then one `ITEM` line per item (its path, `id`, `getCurrentUses()`, `getCondition()`, the bits of `getActualWeight()` and `getSharpness()`, `getCurrentAmmoCount()/getMaxAmmo()`, `getUnequippedWeight()`, `isContainsClip()`, the charge; per class the key id, the radio channel, the alarm time, the fluid amount and the `Food` state: age, off ages, cooked/burnt/rotten, cooking time, hunger, thirst, endurance, boredom, unhappiness, stress, fatigue, flu, sickness, pain, calories, carbohydrates, proteins, lipids, poison power, `freezingTime` and frozen; for a bag its `getCapacity()`, its container's capacity, `getWeightReduction()`, item count, the bits of `getContentsWeight()` and `getUnequippedWeight()` and the explored flag, followed by the lines of its own items, path `n.m.k`); per scenario the generator's counter after the last container (`END ... rand=N`). Scenario `seed:room:container:times[:capacity[:same|fresh[:rooms of the building joined by +[:world]]]]`: `times` containers of that type on a square of a room `room` in a building with the given rooms (default just `room`), filled by `ItemPickerJava.fillContainer` (`same` keeps one room, so its `proceduralSpawnedContainer` counts persist; `fresh`, the default, builds a new one each time); `veh/<VehicleDistributions key>` as room fills from that vehicle distribution's normal room with `fillContainerType` and no square; `corpse/<outfit>` as room fills the container (`inventorymale` or `inventoryfemale`) of an `IsoDeadBody` whose `HumanVisual` outfit is `<outfit>` (the probe builds the body on the cell, sets its square and calls `setContainer`); the optional last field `world` is a comma list of `name=value` with `nights` (`GameTime.nightsSurvived`), `tod` (time of day) or a sandbox option (`MaximumDiminishedLoot`, `DaysUntilMaximumDiminishedLoot`, `ElecShutModifier`, `FoodRotSpeed`, `FridgeFactor`, ...), applied for that scenario and put back after it (the building field must then be given; use the room name). The list (E55: 868 scenarios; E58: the bag pairs, a sweep of every distribution pair, feature-heavy pairs with other seeds and capacities, and the world, zombie and corpse scenarios): 2982 plain scenarios (the room/container pairs of the distributions, their vehicle containers, `same`, capacities 4, 8 and 15, a second room in the building for `forceForRooms`); 1897 with a `world` field (`nights` 3 to 400, `tod` 2.5 and 19, `TimeSinceApo` 2 to 4, `ElecShutModifier` -1 to 20 so the fridges and freezers meet the electricity shut-off, `FoodRotSpeed` 1 to 5, `FridgeFactor` 1 to 5, `MaximumDiminishedLoot` 30 to 70 over 0 to 120 days, so the diminished-loot percentage is above 0); 326 zombie containers (`inventorymale`/`inventoryfemale` with no parent) and corpses of each of the 125 `Outfit_<name>` tables of `Distributions.lua` and of one unknown outfit (294 `corpse/` scenarios). Every scenario of the list runs to the end: each of the 61025 fills is compared whole.

Measured (each is a line of the oracle or of `loot.java.err`):
- `Rand.Next(10000)` per item per roll dominates the draw count (a kitchen fridge: 185 draws with no item, then 3 per item: the item id, the `OutfitRNG` seed `Rand.Next(Integer.MAX_VALUE)` taken around `synchWithVisual`, and `StashSystem.checkStashItem`'s `Rand.Next(100)`).
- A procedural container (most real ones) does nothing unless its `sourceGrid` is set, so the probe gives every container a square (`IsoGridSquare(null, null, x, y, 0)`) in an `IsoRoom`/`IsoBuilding` made without a cell; the `IsoBuilding` constructor draws from `Rand` and is built on a scratch generator, the seeded one put back. The headless square has no objects, no zones, no meta chunk and a `RoomDef` with no bounds: `forceForItems`, `forceForTiles` and `forceForZones` find nothing to force (a `forceForItems` entry still takes its `if` branch); `forceForRooms` draws `Rand.Next(n)` once per listed room that exists in the building (`IsoBuilding.getRandomRoom(String)`).
- Sandbox of the standalone state: `MaximumDiminishedLoot` 20 with `DaysUntilMaximumDiminishedLoot` 3650 (the Apocalypse preset), world age 9:00 of day 0 (`GameTime.getWorldAgeDaysSinceBegin` = 0.0833), so the diminished-loot percentage is 20 * 1 / 3650 = 0; `ZombiePopLootEffect` 0; `AnnotatedMapChance` 4 (no change of the stash chance 60); `reloadDifficulty` 2; `getZombieDensityFactor` is 0 (no player, no meta chunk). Above day 0 the probe sets `GameTime.nightsSurvived`/the time of day and the sandbox options in Java and the port sets its `GameTime` and `SandboxOptions` (`set_by_name`) the same (the `world` field of a scenario): `getCurrentDiminishedLootPercentage` then rises with the world age and `Food.setAutoAge` ages the spawned food by `getWorldAgeDaysSinceBegin`; `GameTime.getMinutesPerDay` is 30.
- `isEmptyFluidContainer` in `doRollItemInternal` is declared outside the loops and never reset: once an `...Empty` item was met every later fluid-container item of that call is emptied (ported as is).
- `DrainableComboItem` chances: `LessFull` items draw `Rand.Next(100) < 80` and, when it fails, the second branch draws again (`< 40`); the amount `randomizeUses` draws twice for `LessFull`.
- `HandWeapon`: the `Rand.Next(100) < 40` of `randomizeGeneralCondition` is drawn for every weapon, but `randomizeCondition` (`Rand.Next(conditionMax + 1)`) runs only when the weapon has a `PhysicsObject` (thrown bombs, `PipeBombSensorV2`); `randomizeFirearmAsLoot` draws `Rand.Next(100) < 90`, then (reload difficulty 2, no magazine) `Rand.Next(100) < 30` and `Rand.Next(1, maxAmmo)`.
- `FluidContainer.readFromScript` draws `FluidContainerScript.getInitialAmount()` twice per item (once in the `&&` test, once for the value) when the amount is a range, and `Rand.Next(size)` for `PickRandomFluid`; `getContentsWeight` of such an item is the fluid amount, which the port only bounds (see below).

- A bag spawned by `ItemSpawner.spawnItem` is filled at once from its own `containers` distribution (`rollContainerItem`, with its junk) and is then filled again by `doRollItemInternal` (`bags` of the container's distribution, or its `containers` distribution one time in `fillRand`); the inner rolls use `rollContainerItemInternal`, which has its own order (no `Rand.Next(100) < 40` before `randomizeGeneralCondition`, no shop test, no cooking, no `rotItem`).
- `ItemContainer.hasRoomFor` of a bag: capacity `min(Capacity, 50)`, `MaxItemSize` refuses a heavier item, and the bag's weight in the container that holds it is checked too (`tryAddItemToContainer`); a bag's `getContentsWeight` is the full weight of its items (the weight reduction applies only when worn).
- `ItemContainer.isItemAllowed` refuses a non-clothing item in a `clothingrack` (unless it has `FitsClothingRack`) after the item was created (its draws are spent) and a bag's `AcceptItemFunction` item (`KeyRing`, `Wallet`, `AmmoStrap_Bullets`, `AmmoStrap_Shells`, `HolsterShoulder`; the five functions of `media/lua/server/Items/AcceptItemFunction.lua` are evaluated natively as stand-ins for the Lua, proven only by the scenarios that reach them).
- Headless, `Clothing.getVisual()` is null (the `ClothingItem` asset is never ready), so `Clothing.addRandomDirt` and its siblings throw a `NullPointerException` in the original (`trashItem` of a clothing item, `clothingwasher` and `clothingdryer` fills): the port returns `not ported` there, since the real game does not throw.
- `Food.setAutoAge` runs for every spawned food: the age is `ageIncrease * FoodRotSpeed` with the fridge branch (`FridgeFactor`) and the freezer branch (once the world age passes `ElecShutModifier` days, `freezingTime` falls by `0.0095999995 * turnToHour * elapsedHours`, `daysFrozen` is the day it thawed; `setFreezingTime` caps at 100 and sets `frozen`, at 0 or less clears it); `Food.isFreezing` needs `isPowered()`, false for a probe container (no parent object), so no probe container is a powered freezer. Both sides print `frz`/`fr`.
- `FluidContainer.getAmount()` snaps to the capacity when `capacity - amount < 0.001` (a cologne bottle with 100 percent initial fluid prints 0.1, not 0.0987); the amount is exact per item now (percent list normalised in `OnScriptsLoaded` unless the script picks a random fluid); the lower/upper bound of the first stage remains only as a fallback for a multi-fluid mix with random percentages, which no item script of the game has.
- `AlarmClock` and `AlarmClockClothing` draw their alarm in `InstanceItem` (a full type with `Classic` in it keeps -1:-1, not set); `Radio` draws `Rand.Next(min, max)` rounded down to a multiple of 200 for its channel (`ZomboidRadio` registered no channel list headless, so no preset and no extra draw); a `Map` item draws `PZArrayUtil.pickRandom` of the `LootMaps.Init` keys (the client Lua that defines `LootMaps` is not loaded headless: the port reads the string keys of that table from the Lua state, "Default" without a draw when none); a `Key` takes its key id (`takeKeyId`) and `ItemPickerJava` removes a building key again (no `BuildingDef` headless) and a car key (no vehicle); a `Moveable` changes the weight of its script once per type and process (`moveables_instanced`: later instances weigh 1.0 in `tryAddItemToContainer`; the probe keeps the set across scenarios and the port is given the same set); `WeaponPart` uses are `floor(1 / useDelta)`.
- Firearms: `randomizeFirearmAsLoot` rolls the `containsClip`/`roundChambered`/attachment state and spawns the magazine and the ammunition box (`magazineType`, `ammoBox`, `ammoType`) into the container; `doGunStorageContainer` and `DoWeaponUpgrade` (the `WeaponUpgrades` table) draw in the original order; `HeadCondition`, `Regional` (`onCreateRegion` with the "General" region: `getSquareRegion` is "General" with no zones), `SpawnCooked`, recorded media (`RecordedMedia` registered nothing headless, so no draw), the trash/worn/rotten flags, `spawnWith` (`copyClothing` fails on the missing visual before it changes anything), cooking of spawned food and `NeverEmpty` follow the Java statement order. `ApplyOwnerName` names the item only for an `IsoDeadBody` that has a descriptor; neither the container of a room nor the probe's corpse (no `SurvivorDesc`) has one.
- A zombie container (`inventorymale`/`inventoryfemale`, which `ItemContainer.isCorpse` tests by type) takes its own branch of `fillContainerInternal`: the bags it holds first (the `bags` of the outfit table unless the bag has `BagsFillException`, else its own `containers` table one time in `fillRand`), then the table `Outfit_<getOutfitName()>` of the `all` room (the outfit name needs an `IsoDeadBody` parent; with none the key is `Outfit_inventorymale`) and, unless that table sets `defaultInventoryLoot` false, the `inventorymale`/`inventoryfemale` table of `all` (a skeleton `IsoDeadBody` is not filled at all; the probe's corpse is never one). `ItemContainer.isItemAllowed` refuses an item whose type contains `Corpse` in a body's container, and a bag lying directly in it is not asked for room.

Ported: `ItemPickerJava` (`fillContainer`, `fillContainerType`, `fillContainerTypeInternal`, the zombie/corpse branch of `fillContainerInternal`, `rollItem`, `rollProceduralItemInternal` with `getDistribInHashMap` in `HashMap` order, `doRollItemInternal`, `rollContainerItem`/`rollContainerItemInternal`, `itemSpawnSanityCheck`, `tryAddItemToContainer`, the key bookkeeping, `onCreateRegion`, `getActualSpawnChance`, `getLootModifier`, `getLootType`, `getLootModifierFromType`, `doSandboxSettings`, `NO_GENERIC_LOOT_CONTAINERS`), `ItemSpawner.spawnItem(String, ItemContainer)`, `InventoryItemFactory.createItemInternal`, the draw path of `Item.InstanceItem` (`Clothing` palette, colour, dirt, blood and holes up to the missing visual, `FluidContainer.readFromScript`, the `OutfitRNG` seed, the model index of `synchWithVisual`, the alarm, radio channel, map and key branches), `ItemContainer.hasRoomFor`/`floatingPointCorrection`/`getContentsWeight`/`isItemAllowed`, `Food` (`getActualWeight`, `setAutoAge` with the fridge and freezer branches, `setFreezingTime`, `setCooked`, cooking of spawned food), `DrainableComboItem` (`getMaxUses`, `updateWeight`, `randomizeUses`), `HandWeapon` (`randomizeFirearmAsLoot`, `doGunStorageContainer`, `DoWeaponUpgrade`, head condition), `StashSystem.checkStashItem` (the draw; no item here names a stash), `SandboxOptions.getCurrentDiminishedLootPercentage` and `lootItemRemovalListContains`; the script side `FluidContainerScript` (capacity, initial percentages and amount range, `PickRandomFluid`, fluid count) in `Item`. Item classes: `Normal` (ComboItem), `Food`, `Literature`, `Drainable`, `Weapon`, `Clothing`, `Container` (`InventoryContainer` with its own `ItemContainer`), `WeaponPart`, `Key`, `Radio`, `Moveable`, `AlarmClock`, `AlarmClockClothing`, `Map`; the components `FluidContainer`, `Durability` and `ContextMenuConfig`.

UNKNOWN / not reproduced (each is a guard that returns `Err("not ported: ...")`; no scenario of the list reaches one: E55 ended with 1155 of the 1938 room/container pairs running to the end (30 fills each from seed 3, a scan on the Rust side not kept in the repo), E58 stage 1 (bags) with 1282, `Key`/`Radio`/`Moveable`/`AlarmClock`/`Map`/`WeaponPart` and the item flags with 1581 and 1866, the stash roll and exact fluid amounts with all 1938; the 4491 `world` variants of those run to the end too):
- Item classes outside the 14 above (the gate `item class ...`), a component other than `FluidContainer`, `Durability` and `ContextMenuConfig`, two `FluidContainer` blocks, a `FluidContainer` with a whitelist or blacklist together with an initial fluid, several initial fluids that mix with no random pick (no script of the game has one), an `OnCooked` function other than `RecipeCodeOnCooked.cannedFood`, recorded media with data (`RecordedMedia` registers nothing headless: CDs and VHS take the no-media branch), a `NeverEmpty` bag with `spawnWith`, `Food.setCurrentUses` (`consumeHunger`), `randomizeGeneralCondition` with `ConditionMax` 0, a head condition of 0 (`setCondition(0)` plays a sound), a spawned item id equal to one already in the container, a sandbox option the port's `SandboxOptions` lacks.
- Not measured against a running game (the headless state has none): the Moveable `PickUpWeight` from sprites (weights use the script), `Radio` channel lists and presets (`ZomboidRadio` registers none), the `LootMaps` definitions of the client Lua (only the key list is read), a stash item (`doStashItem`) and the stash annotation with a loaded map (`getMetaGrid().getRoomAt` is null, so no stash can be picked), the tables of `RecordedMedia`, the visual copied by `copyClothing` (tint, texture, decals, dirt, blood, holes: `getVisual()` is null), the owner an `IDcard` takes from a corpse's `SurvivorDesc`, `Food.isFreezing` in a powered freezer (`isPowered` needs a parent object), the vehicle `specific` rooms and `fillContainer` of a vehicle part, the `OnFillContainer` event (its only handler, `LootLog.lua`, returns at once without `ISLootLog`; the port does not fire it), `initialiseItem` (game thread only), `ItemConfigurator` (no item script has an item config) and a non-zero `getZombieDensityFactor` (no player, no meta chunk; `ZombiePopLootEffect` 0).
- Weights: a `FluidContainer` item's `getContentsWeight` is the fluid amount, exact now for one initial fluid; the lower/upper bound path stays for a mix with random percentages and `hasRoomFor`, the weight of a weapon or a bag returns an error when the amount is only bounded (never in the list). `HashMap` order for `getDistribInHashMap` uses `java::hash_map_order` (bins of 8 or more keys with 64 or more buckets, which the JDK treeifies, may iterate differently; the procedural lists have at most 12 keys).
- Stage c (full item objects): `LootItem` holds the script, `id`, uses (`Food.getCurrentUses` from the hunger), condition, actual weight, sharpness, ammunition, clip, charge, key id, radio channel, alarm, fluid amount and the whole `Food` state, all compared. Not compared: the clothing visual and colours, `ItemPickInfo`, mod data (for example the `printMedia` of a region newspaper and the new name of a spawned newspaper), the model index beyond its draw, and the real `InventoryItem` subclasses (the save-oriented `InventoryItem` of `inventory_item.rs` is not filled by the roll).


## Sound scripts by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh sounds` → `OK   sounds: 160014 lines identical`. The booted original server (`SoundsProbe` in `ServerHarness`) against the port (`pz-dump sounds`). Compared: all 3036 `sound` scripts of `media/scripts` (`ScriptManager.getAllGameSounds`, in its order: per module the `HashMap` order of the names kept; all in module Base, none dropped) with every field of `GameSound` and `GameSoundClip` by reflection in declaration order (floats with `Float.toString`) and the values derived from them (`getMaxDistanceOfClips`, `getMasterName`, `isLooped`, `getUserVolume`, `hasMinDistance`, `hasMaxDistance`, `isStopImmediate`, `getEffectiveVolume`), each script's module, `enabled`, `debugOnly`, script version and body count; then the `GameSounds` registry `ScriptsLoaded` filled (the `sounds` list in order, `soundByName` by sorted key as the position of its sound in the list, `soundIsPaused`, `getCategories()` and `getSoundsInCategory` of each of the 10 categories); the 5 `soundTimeline` scripts (`getAllSoundTimelines`: `eventName` and `positionByName` by sorted key) and the 4 `vehicleEngineRPM` scripts (`getAllVehicleEngineRPMs`: name and the eight `rpmData` slots). Then 120 crafted commands (`tests/sounds_cases.txt`, generated content only) run on the emptied registry, the exception (`toString`) and the state after each: `GameSoundScript.Load`, `GameSoundScript.reset`, `SoundTimelineScript.Load`/`getPosition`/`reset`, `VehicleEngineRPM.Load`/`reset`, `addSound`, `getSound`/`getOrCreateSound`, `isKnownSound`, `getSoundsInCategory`, `setUserVolume`/`getUserVolume` (with `SystemDisabler.setEnableAdvancedSoundOptions`), `getRandomClip` (the generator replaced by `new Random(seed)`), `loadINI` over crafted `sounds.ini` files (written into the original's cache folder and the port's scratch folder) and `Reset`.

Measured on the real scripts: `BaseSoundBank.instance` is null on the booted server and `SystemDisabler.getEnableAdvancedSoundOptions()` is false; no clip has an FMOD event description (`initClipEvents` does nothing when `GameServer.server`), so `getUserVolume` is always 1 and `getEffectiveVolume` is the clip's volume; nothing called `getSound` before the server was up (no "AUTO" sound in the registry). All 3036 sounds have one clip (3035 with an `event`, one with a `file`), priority 5 (`Load` never sets it), `is3D` true, user volume 1; 32 loop, `maxInstancesPerEmitter` is 2 for 22 and -1 for the others, `master` is Primary (2986), Ambient (31), VehicleEngine (18) or Music (1); 278 clips have a `distanceMax` (init flag 2); one clip sets `volume` (0.5) and none sets `distanceMin`, `pitch`, `reverbFactor`, `reverbMaxRange` or `stopImmediate`, so those paths are proven by the crafted cases only. Two sounds have a second body (`ResetExisting`: `GameSoundScript.reset()` runs before it, so `reloadEpoch` is 1 on the sound and its clip).

Measured quirks (each a line of the oracle): `GameSoundScript.Load` sets `gameSound.name` first, so a body that throws leaves a named sound with what was read before the throw (the bucket then drops the script); every value of the sound or clip block is `value.string.split("=")` and `ss[1]` is read at once, so a value without `=`, with nothing after it (`category=`) or with only `=`s throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` (`Index 1 out of bounds for length 1`, and `Index 0 out of bounds for length 0` for a value that is `==`), also for `enabled`/`debugOnly`/`loop` and unknown keys; `a=b=c` gives `b`; an empty key is ignored; the last value of a block without a trailing comma is dropped; `//` comments are not stripped in `Load` (only by `LoadFile`: the text becomes part of the key and the value is ignored); `master` is `Enum.valueOf` (case-sensitive: `No enum constant zombie.audio.GameSound.MasterVolume.music`); `distanceMin/Max` are `Integer.parseInt` (a decimal, an empty value or 2147483648 throw `NumberFormatException: For input string: "..."`) stored as float (16777217 gives 1.6777216E7) and set their init flag only after parsing; `pitch`, `volume`, `reverbFactor`, `reverbMaxRange` are `Float.parseFloat` (`1e1`, `.5`, `5f`, `2.5D`, `NaN`, `Infinity`, `-0` accepted; an empty value is `NumberFormatException: empty String`); `loop`/`is3D` are `parseBoolean` (any case of true); `maxInstancesPerEmitter` is `tryParseInt` with -1; the body's own name is ignored (`load(name, ...)` decides). `addSound` of a name already in `soundByName` replaces the first sound of that name in `sounds` at its place; `getOrCreateSound` files an unknown name as an "AUTO" sound with one empty clip under the name without ".wav" and ".ogg", so a name ending in either is never found again (every call adds a new sound), and a later `addSound` of the stripped name finds the key but no sound of that name in the list and throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException: Index N out of bounds for length N` with nothing changed. `setUserVolume` clamps to 0..2 and keeps NaN. `loadINI` (`ConfigFile.read` of `<cache dir>/sounds.ini`): lines trimmed, `#` and blank lines skipped, a line without `=` only logged, `Version=n` sets the version (a non-number is only logged), keys are not trimmed (`A2 = 1` names `A2 `, not found), the value is `ss[1]` (`A=b=c` gives `b`, `A=` gives ""), applied only when the version is at most 1 and with `tryParseFloat(value, 1.0F)` (a non-number or empty value resets to 1); a line of only `=`s, `Version=` or an empty key (`IllegalArgumentException`) ends the read with false and nothing is applied (the options read before it are kept in the `ConfigFile` but `loadINI` ignores them). `VehicleEngineRPM.Load`: `VERSION` 0 or 1 is required (`unknown vehicleEngineRPM VERSION "2"`, and `"vehicleEngineRPM"`, the block type, when it is missing), every child must be `data` (at most 8), a data value must be `gearChange` or `afterGearChange` (`tryParseFloat`, 0 on a bad number), `reset` zeroes the slots without removing them. `SoundTimelineScript.Load`: every value is a marker `tryParseInt(value, 0)` (also `enabled` and an empty key); `getPosition` of an unknown marker is -1.

Ported: `zombie.scripting.objects.GameSoundScript` (`Load`, `LoadClip`, `reset`), `zombie.audio.GameSound` (defaults, `MasterVolume`, `setUserVolume`, `getUserVolume`, `getRandomClip`, `getMaxDistanceOfClips`, `reset`) and `GameSoundClip` (defaults, the init flags, `getEffectiveVolume`), `zombie.GameSounds` (`addSound`, `isKnownSound`, `getSound`, `getOrCreateSound`, `getCategories`, `getSoundsInCategory`, `ScriptsLoaded`, `loadINI`, `Reset`), `zombie.config.ConfigFile.read` with the `StringConfigOption` it creates, `zombie.scripting.objects.SoundTimelineScript`, `zombie.vehicles.VehicleEngineRPM` and `EngineRPMData`, and the three script buckets in `ScriptManager` (`sounds`, `sound_timelines`, `vehicle_engine_rpms`, with the `game_sounds` registry after `ScriptsLoaded`). `PZMath.tryParseFloat`, `Math.max(float, float)`. The generic bucket loader (`load_bucket`, E49) loads all three.

UNKNOWN / not ported: everything that needs FMOD or a client. `GameSounds.initClipEvents` and the event, `.ogg` and `.wav` lookups of `addSound`/`getOrCreateSound` (they run only off the server, and with `BaseSoundBank.instance instanceof FMODSoundBank`, which a standalone or `-nosound` run does not have), the debug-only unused-event report of `ScriptsLoaded` (`Core.debug`), `loadNonBankSounds`, `OnReloadSound`, `saveINI`, the sound preview, `fix3DListenerPosition`, `GameSound.numClipsUsingParameter`, `GameSoundClip.hasSustainPoints`/`hasParameter`/`getEventDescription`/`checkReloaded` (hot reload), the `FMOD_STUDIO_EVENT_DESCRIPTION` fields (always null here and printed as null), and `zombie.audio.parameters.*` / `FMODParameter*` / `FMODAmbientWalls` / `ObjectAmbientEmitters` / `SoundInstanceLimiter` (they read FMOD state at run time). `ScriptManager::load` has no cache folder, so it runs `GameSounds.ScriptsLoaded` without its closing `loadINI`: a caller with a cache folder runs `game_sounds.load_ini(dir)` (the oracle's server has no `sounds.ini` at start). `addSound` of a sound without a name (never made by a script) is UNKNOWN (`Err`). Number parsing keeps the limits of `java::parse_int` (ASCII digits only) and `java::parse_float` (no hexadecimal floats), neither is in the vanilla data. `ScriptModule.disabled` modules are not modelled (as for the other buckets). The music tables of E41 (`MusicIntensityConfig`, `MusicThreatConfig`) are filled by Lua and were already ported then.

## Mod loading by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh mods` → `OK   mods: 1627 lines identical`. The original standalone (`ModsProbe`, a `game_case`: `setCacheDir(<scratch>/java)`, `ZomboidFileSystem.init`, `Languages.init`) against the port (`pz-dump mods`, cache dir `<scratch>/rust`, made afresh). Both run the commands of `tests/mods_cases.txt` (generated content only): they first write 29 crafted mod folders under the cache dir's `mods` and 7 outside it (`ws/Contents/mods/...`, `ws/<number>/mods/...`) plus `mods/default.txt`, then call the original API: `GameVersion.parse`, `getGameVersionIntFromName`, `getModVersionDirName`, `setModFoldersOrder`/`getAllModFolders` (orders `mods`, `mods,mods,workshop,steam`, `steam`), `ChooseGameInfo.getModDetails`/`getAvailableModDetails`/`readModInfo`, `ActiveMods.getById`/`setModActive`/`isModActive`/`removeMod`/`checkMissingMods`, `loadMods(list)`, `loadMods("default")` (reads `default.txt`), `loadMods("Custom")`, `getString`, `getDirectoryString`, `isKnownFile`, `getAbsolutePath`, with `Translator.setLanguage` EN then FR. Printed: every result, the state of `ChooseGameInfo` (`Mods`, `MissingMods`, the shared `tempStrings`) and of the file system (`modIdToDir`, `modDirToMod`, `mods`, `modFolders`, `modFoldersOrder`), every `ActiveMods` list and `s_loaded`, the file map's size, `allAbsolutePaths`' size and its entries that point into the cache dir, and at the end every `ChooseGameInfo.Mod` of `modDirToMod` with all its fields (the folders `baseFile`/`mediaFile`/`actionGroupsFile`/`animSetsFile`/`animsXFile` as absolute and canonical paths, posters, require/incompatible/loadAfter/loadBefore, name, desc, id, url, author, modVersion, icon, category, workshopId, source, availableDone/available, versionMin/Max, packs with flags, tiledefs, read, valid). Paths print as `$S` (cache dir) and `$G` (game folder). `cargo test --test oracle_hashes mods` reruns `pz-dump mods` under `target/tmp`. The golden is for this machine's file system (APFS, case-insensitive): the mod folders are listed in directory order (both sides list the same names), and one case relies on `Common/` answering for `common/`.

Measured (each a line of the oracle): a mod folder counts when it has `common/mod.info` or `<version folder>/mod.info`; `examplemod` (any case) is never listed; a `mod.info` that is a folder is listed and then fails to read. The version folder is, of the names in the mod folder (files too), the last listed of those with the highest `getGameVersionIntFromName` from 42.0 to 42.21 (`42.5` over `41`/`42`/`43`; `42.21` and `42.21.7` tie at 42021 and the later listed wins; `+42` counts as 42; a file named `42.9` is picked and `mod.info` then comes from `common`); none: `42.0`, even when missing. `mod.info` is read as UTF-8 without dropping a BOM (a BOM line `id=...` is ignored, a BOM `name=` keeps U+FEFF in the name; CRLF and bare CR end lines; a bad byte becomes U+FFFD); each line goes to the first key it *contains* (`id=`, `versionMin=`, `versionMax=` only at the start), so `url=https://pzwiki.net/name=x` sets the name to `url=https://pzwiki.net/x`; descriptions concatenate; `require=` with nothing makes the list `[""]`, which makes the mod unavailable; `\` is dropped from id lists; a url outside Steam/projectzomboid/theindiestone/pzwiki is not kept; a poster or icon not in the version folder is taken from `common` even when missing there. Invalid (the mod is `null`, its fields as far as read stay): `pack=` with no name, `pack=abtype=ui` (no space: the StringIndexOutOfBoundsException), `tiledef=` without two fields, with a non-number or outside 100..8189, a `versionMax`/`versionMin` that does not parse or has a minor above 999, a `Mod.json` translation that is not JSON. Pack flags: 68 (compression option 4 | 64), `.floor` 64, `type=ui` 2, another type keeps 68. `Translator.readModTranslation` reads `Mod.json` of the language stack from common then the version folder (FR after EN: a FR file with only a description changes only that). Two folders with one id: the one earlier in `getAllModFolders` keeps the id (`ModY` stays with its own folder although `ModN` claims it too); a mod read from outside the mod folders (index -1) takes the id over (`DupWs` becomes `ModE`). The source is `Workshop` when the folder two levels up is `Contents` (any case, after `Paths.normalize`: `Contents/mods/../mods//WsMod` too), `Steam` with that name as workshop id when it is a Steam id (0..2^64-1: `18446744073709551615` yes, `18446744073709551616` and `-5` no). Availability: `versionMin` 42.30 or `versionMax` 42.10 make a mod unavailable, both at 42.21 do not; a missing required mod makes it unavailable; the require cycle `ModP`↔`ModQ` is available. `tempStrings` is shared: after a lookup that lists the folders it holds the folder list, not the ids seen. `loadMods` orders required mods first (`[ModB, ModC, ModG]` with ModC unavailable → `[ModA, ModB, ModG]`), `checkMissingMods` drops unavailable ids from the end, `default.txt` is read with its lines joined (`MOD = ModB ,` counts, `mod = ,` and `map = ...` in `mods` do not). `loadMod` puts each file of the common folder, then of the version folder, into the file map under its path relative to that folder: a mod's `media/newtiledefinitions.tiles` shadows the game's (case-insensitive lookups), the later mod wins (`ModB` over `ModA`), files at a mod's top become top-level names (`getString("icon.png")` finds ModA's icon), folders under `media/maps/` are entries without a trailing '/' (found only as `media/maps/modamap/`), and a version or common folder that does not exist is itself the entry `""` (`getString("")` returns it). `ActiveMods.getById` trims for the lookup but creates the list under the id as given (`" Other "` and `"Other"` become two lists); a whitespace id is the IllegalArgumentException.

Ported: `zombie.gameStates.ChooseGameInfo` (`getModDetails`, `getAvailableModDetails`, `readModInfo`, `readModInfoAux`, `getMinRequiredVersion`, `Reset`), `ChooseGameInfo.Mod` (constructor with the version folder, `PZModFolder`s and source; `isAvailable`, `isAvailableSelf`, `isAvailableRequired`, getters, `setUrl`), `PackFile`, `TileDef`; `zombie.ZomboidFileSystem` mod side (`setCacheDir`, `getCacheDir(Sub)`, `getModVersionDirName`, `getGameVersionIntFromName`, `getAllModFolders(Aux)`, `setModFoldersOrder`, `resetModFolders`, `setModIdToDir`, `getModDir`, `getModInfoForDir`, `loadMod`, `loadModAndRequired`, `loadModsAux`, `loadMods(List)`, `loadMods(String)` outside a client, `readDefaultModsTxt`, `getModIDs`, `getRelativeFile(URI, String)`, `getDirectoryString`, `isKnownFile`, `getAbsolutePath`, `Reset`, `PZFolder`, `PZModFolder`) and `File.getCanonicalPath` (`JDK_Canonicalize`); `zombie.modding.ActiveMods` (statics and instance methods but `renderUI`, `checkMissingMaps`), `ActiveModsFile.read`/`fromString`; `zombie.core.GameVersion`; `Translator.readModTranslation`. `getRelativeFile` now relativizes as `URI.relativize` does for any root (the game folder's own path gives "", with a '/' kept from the argument); `fonts`, `animsets`, `translator`, `luaload` rerun identical.

UNKNOWN / not ported: the Steam folders (`workshop`, `steam` in `-modfolders` need Steam mode; standalone they add nothing, as measured), the file watchers, `loadModPackFiles`/`loadModTileDefs` (the packs and tiledefs are parsed only), the client's translation-only mods (`loadTranslationMods`), `ActiveModsFile.write` (so `mods/loaded.txt` and `saveModsFile` give an `Err`), `checkMissingMaps` (`MapGroups`; a list with maps gives an `Err`), `isModded`, `getWorkshopItemMods`, `searchForModInfo`, the poster texture (`Mod.getTexture`). `loadModAfter`/`loadModBefore`/`incompatible` are only parsed: the Java side never applies them (the Lua mod selector does, `ModOrderListBox.lua`, not ported). A require cycle passed to `loadMods` recurses without end in the original (StackOverflowError by reading the code; not run). Unicode digits in a Steam id or in `tiledef=`/`Integer.parseInt` are not taken as digits here (Java takes them). Not measured: a case-sensitive file system, a cache dir behind a symlink, `getString`'s `relativeMap` cache (not kept; a cache).

## Weather and climate by direct port (2026-11-16)
`sh tools/oracle.sh climate` → `OK   climate: 306807 lines identical` (about 56 s). The original `zombie.iso.weather.ClimateManager` runs inside the booted dedicated server (`ClimateProbe` in `ServerHarness`) against the port (`pz-dump climate`). The probe: the global `Rand` (`RandStandard`) is swapped for a `java.util.Random(seed)` (the draw counter `id` is printed, so the number of draws is compared too); `GameTime` is set to the scenario date and hour (60 minutes per day, multiplier 1, fps multiplier 2, so `getMultiplier()` is 1.6 on a server); the erosion season (the object `ClimateManager.init` takes from `ErosionMain`) is moved by `setDay` the way `ErosionMain.mainTimer` does on a new date; then `new ClimateManager()` (the server's `ClimateMain.lua` handler runs), `init(metaGrid)`, and per tick the clock core of `GameTime.update` (time of day, nights at 7:00, midnight `advanceOneDay`, minute stamp, ten-minute mark with `updateEveryTenMins`) followed by `ClimateManager.update()`. Scenarios are `seed:year:month:day:hour:days[:options]` (month and day 0-based); the JVM's zone is pinned to UTC. Printed: the set-up (sandbox options, game mode, map bounds), the colours, all 36 season colours, the 40 forecasts after `init` and after every day change (values, fronts, dummy periods), and at every climate tick (each game minute) all 13 climate floats (final and internal), colours, the snow bool, the air masses, snow state, fog, moon phase, view distance, the current front, the weather period (header when it starts or stops, every stage, the running stage) and the thunder clouds and, when it changes, the thunder event the server would send. Floats and doubles are printed with `Float.toString`/`Double.toString`.

Scenarios (all in `tools/oracle.sh` and `tests/oracle_hashes.rs`): a summer week; four spring days with storm stages and running thunder clouds; five winter days with a blizzard; five days with a tropical storm; two days each of `ClimateCycle` 6 (endless blizzard), 3 with `FogCycle` 3 and `Rain` 5, 2 with `FogCycle` 2 and `Rain` 1, and 5 (endless snow); `DayNightCycle` 2 and 3; and the game modes Winter is Coming (5 days), The Descending Fog and A Storm is Coming (the last only sets a flag in `ClimateManager`).

Ported: `ClimateManager` (`AirFront`, `ClimateFloat`/`ClimateColor`/`ClimateBool`, `DayInfo`, `SeasonColor`, `update`, `updateSandboxOverrides`, `updateValues`, `updateSnow`, view distance, `CalculateWeatherFrontStrength`, `load`), `ClimateValues`, `ClimateForecaster` (the forecast objects live in an arena so the shifting array and the overlap references alias as in Java), `ClimateMoon`, `WeatherPeriod` (pattern creation with the period's own `Random(1984)` reseeded per date, stage creation and pooling, `update`, `updateCurrentStage`, `Collections.shuffle`), `ThunderStorm` (`startThunderCloud`, `setCenter`, `update`, `triggerThunderEvent` on a server), `SimplexNoise`, `ErosionSeason` with `Noise2D`, `GameTime` (`getMultiplier`, the clock core, `AnimTimer`), `GregorianCalendar` fields and `add`, `Random.setSeed`.

Measured on the way:
